# Chapter Summaries

## Volume 01 — Chapter Summaries

### Chapter 1 — The Dead Track
Elias Rook begins an ordinary Kestrel signal-and-door inspection and finds a train moving on a decommissioned track, a service door with handles on both sides, a clock repeating the same minute, and Nell’s direct warning from inside the station. He establishes a physical retreat line, identifies the changing load path, and enters the service landing under ordinary procedures. The carriage appears behind an impossible wall, and a woman’s voice calls Elias from inside it.

### Chapter 2 — Keep One Breath
The collapse leaves fifteen Car 3 passengers trapped inside the buckled carriage, with Tarin Voss unresponsive and pinned beneath a steel handrail driven through his lower chest. Elias reads future Mara’s Still Hand offer aloud, records the present Mara’s limited emergency-treatment consent, and accepts the one-body loan for Tarin while Mara repairs the injury. The gift keeps Tarin alive but does not heal him; the operation creates an unmapped service door as aftermath, while Elias loses sensation in his left hand.

### Chapter 3 — The Door Everyone Used
With the Still Hand still active, Mara sets the movement order for the Car 3 survivors and Elias delegates rope work, movement decisions, and mechanical anchoring rather than trying to control everyone himself. The changed service door remains unstable and depends on ordinary braces, lines, and judgment; passengers remember incompatible versions of the corridor. The last survivors cross, the door narrows into a dark seam, and the terminal and paper map disagree about whether the route remains.

### Chapter 4 — The Record of a Rescue
Elias helps move Group A into surface triage and discovers that Joon’s manifest assigned sixteen Car 3 seats while the physical carriage count is fifteen. He records measurements, tool marks, conflicting memories, and raw audio, then admits that he did not disclose the branch-specific afterimage that may affect his judgment. Mara keeps her consent limited to present treatment, while Joon preserves copies. A live rail-engineer signal from Nell reaches them with a warning that the north stair is a branch, not an exit.

### Chapter 5 — The North Stair
Elias, Mara, Tomas, and Joon follow the branch-crossed signal through the still-active operation, refusing to ask future Mara to extend the one-body gift when Oren Pike is found alive in a maintenance pocket. Future Mara’s route memory is explicitly partial, and Elias chooses thermal imaging, rope, shoring, and a breadcrumb trail over a faster pursuit of Nell’s signal. Oren reaches the refuge, but the north stair and a second possible door disappear before they can be mapped.

### Chapter 6 — When the Third Wheel Stops
At the temporary refuge, Groups A and B bring the physical count to sixteen. The maintenance trolley’s third wheel stops, the Still Hand returns automatically, and Elias does not bargain for more time; the service door becomes structurally unusable and drops out of the immediate route index, although its physical absence is not proven. Joon and Tomas preserve measurements and paper records, and an unidentified male voice emerges from the altered wall and raw audio warning, “Do not make the map agree.”

### Chapter 7 — No Answer
In the surface triage yard, Elias tests whether the completed Still Hand exchange can be reopened and receives a deliberate `No answer`, not a relay failure. The raw relay carries a possible-Elias voice asking him to preserve contradictory paper and audio rather than force agreement; Elias refuses to treat the voice as proof of a faction. The team builds a battery-backed communications setup, duplicate maps, and a consent-recording anchor lamp while accepting that no supernatural capacity is available.

### Chapter 8 — The Office at the Door
Sable Arden arrives with a credible safety argument for sealing Kestrel but a form already classifying the event as equipment failure before the first rail alarm. Elias gives copies while keeping the original notebook and raw recording, while Mara and Joon retain control of the medical and communications records. Inez Sato offers local power and shelter only after residents consent, can withdraw, and keep local control; the team’s first refuge begins as a conditional civic partnership. A thermal survey finds two weak signatures behind a wall the terminal calls empty, and Nell’s signal repeats.

### Chapter 9 — The People Behind the Wall
With no active Still Hand, Elias, Mara, Tomas, and Joon reach Group C and extract seven people from the altered service alcove using ordinary braces, rope, thermal tools, and oxygen. Future Mara briefly answers Elias’s request for an extension but deliberately refuses to reopen the completed exchange or cover more than one body; Elias accepts the refusal and gives up the cleanest route toward Nell. Nadi Bell and Ansel reach the refuge, the paper count reaches twenty-three, and the Civic Spine reports zero Car 3 passengers despite the physical rescue.

### Chapter 10 — The Names on Paper
The team moves the twenty-three survivors into Inez’s Cinder Quarter refuge, where residents approve power circuits, record refusals, and retain withdrawal rights while a prototype uses batteries, lamps, seam glass, maps, and barriers. Sable presses for an equipment-failure report; Mara establishes a rule for disclosing every future offer and refusal, and Elias writes a reply to Nell, does not send it, and does not claim that the signal is a route home. Joon finds the pre-collapse route entry’s second internal maintenance code, `MNT-4C-17`, and the team decides to compare the records before deciding whether another entry is justified; two deeper heat signatures remain.

### Chapter 11 — What the Survivors Need
The morning after the move is spent on antibiotics, mats, one oxygen cylinder, and a demand for a consolidated list that nobody will fill. Elias works the care line rather than the plan, announces every time his left hand fails, and hands each job to someone else. Tarin Voss is told the whole truth about the loan and asks for his shoes. Niko Senn asks that his name not be published and dictates the wording. Oren Pike asks for a photograph of his own chart. Ansel Duarte refuses to be photographed or argued with. Nadi Bell asks whether she may make a call and is told that every line is logged. Mara makes the antibiotic decision without Elias in it. The twenty-three stop being a count, and Joon clears the long table so the records can be compared where they can be seen.

### Chapter 12 — The Map Under Dispute
The long table becomes a hearing room in front of the twenty-three. Elias cannot draw the door to measurement, says so out loud, and hands the pencil to Joon. Four survivors remember incompatible corridors and are told, without being disbelieved, that the conflict is recorded and not resolved. Anja Ro identifies herself as the woman in the orange coat. The Civic Spine offers a `Suggested correction` that would delete the 05:42–06:18 entries to make the index agree; it is refused, photographed, and signed. The room hears the raw possible-Elias audio. The page that results has three columns — unsafe, shifted, physically absent — only the first of which is proven. A 1600 site-archive notice makes the 05:17 paper route book the immediate clock.

### Chapter 13 — MNT-4C-17
At the west apron relay, the paper log has already been lifted from its felt slot by a hand that knew the clip, and the card reader can be read only once. The single read returns a route entry for sector 4C under `MNT-4C-17`, sequence four, at 05:17:11. Tomas explains the credential format and its pre-2035 class-three limits, which include an unindexed local route entry and no authority over alarms or passenger records. A mechanical fault tape shows two ticks before any alarm, 05:17.11 and 05:24.40, and the team takes a graphite impression rather than removing the tape. Joon argues the code off the public wall and gets a formulation that is true and accuses no one. Three copies go out, one of them into a residents’ lock-box, and the roster that could name a holder turns out to be in a building they cannot enter.

### Chapter 14 — The Form That Was Ready
The site’s paper records go into Office crates at 1600 with a clerk writing **LOG ABSENT PRIOR TO TRANSFER** on the transfer sheet. The next morning’s supervised exchange in the hall produces faithful copies and one refusal: the notebook stays with the team, and the Office is offered a supervised reading instead. Sable gives a real administrative explanation for the 05:24 timestamp — a standing classification pack for a designated seam site and a genuine 04:50 patrol fault — and then produces the dispatch sheet showing a Continuity team signed out for 4C at 05:02, with a filled second signature on the original and a holder field the 2035 renumbering never carried forward. Mara dictates the full loan record, including the borrower’s undisclosed afterimage and the refusal of an extension, and makes the Office sign it. Eleven witness statements are taken with their conditions attached. Sable offers a controlled re-entry for the originals; Elias declines, and Sable says her form is an indictment of everyone who came before her.

### Chapter 15 — The Local Vote
Inez calls a meeting and refuses to let Elias brief it. Residents speak first: an unasked laundrette power bank, a request to move the Office truck off a street, and a refrigeration tech who wants the crew gone by Friday because his whole life has been reorganized around a hole in the ground. Cards go round per person, including per crew member. Thirty-one consent, nine refusal, four abstain, one card filled twice. Every refusal is read aloud, including a father’s stated preference about his daughter sleeping. Seven conditions are agreed, including that a blackout means the team goes dark too. The authority split is written on the hall wall with Elias’s line — physical movement, and no sole command — added by him and tapped twice in chalk. Nadi Bell closes the chapter by pricing what a phone call about her sister will cost.

### Chapter 16 — The Bell Row
On Lena Mir’s loan of the clinic wall for the hour between nine and ten, with a two-minute warning given partway through, Yolanda Bell remembers signing the west apron run sheets and recites the series 9-1-7-4 from memory, and the carbon stub in Nadi’s hands carries the same four digits. Yolanda has done her own arithmetic: the official list says four people from that building and there were five. At the transit operations desk, clerk Bex Amadi confirms the stub physically and freezes any query that touches the incident, so a routine dispatch confirmation is pending in nine days. **R. Adare**, a polite broker, offers to restore the digital row, sell an unlogged line, and move seven people out of the Quarter that night using the seven of them as anchors. Nadi refuses and has the refusal written and signed; Elias does not offer her sister’s name and loses the cleaner argument for it. The file is copied into five buildings, and Elias records that a missing row is a civic injury rather than a puzzle.

### Chapter 17 — The Same Relay
Tomas asks for a conversation out of earshot and admits that the 2034 collapse began with the same east-leg draw and the same slab cycle, over a buried paired node under the west apron that was written off in 2035 with his signature second on the page. He was on the 2029 panel that wrote the one-voice safety rule, he signed the reroute that left Samuel Rook holding a frame, and he has been giving Elias the softer version of it for eight years. He admits the rule was written by frightened competent people to stop a real thing, that his caution may be part of the problem, and that he has not yet earned the right to say so. He offers a five-name, refusable, expiring stop plan and two lines he will not cross again. Elias asks for the expiry date so the plan cannot become a procedure with his name on it.

### Chapter 18 — The Deeper Heat
A six-hour, hard-cut survey of the west apron and lower junction finds a 2031 repair pour running eleven meters and terminating six meters short of the camera target, plus a brick lining at seven meters on the bore line, and a gas curve that reads like a sealed room rather than a fire. **Halla Dren**, a shaft and outfall foreman nineteen years ahead, answers with `No loan offered` and `Warning: the branch is changing`, lends nothing, gives the location of the pour, and asks that the shaft not be named in any consolidated list because a named location becomes one to be closed. Joon records the request and refuses it in the same hand on the same page. A person behind the wall answers three knocks in the code a school safety week taught Elias. At 12:40 the slab moves four millimeters on Tomas’s pre-written threshold and Elias, not Tomas, calls the stop. They return with a sounding map, a gas curve, a camera disagreement, a rebar coupon, and a recording of knocking.

### Chapter 19 — The Seal at the Gate
The Office arrives with a containment order, a seal crew, and a survey van, and Sable brings two things: a 2031 contractor survey putting a migrating void two hundred and ten meters from the clinic, and the 2019 Salt Crossing record in which an unlicensed crew opened a shaft and six people died. She also brings the crew sheet — a Continuity team signed out for 4C at 05:02, a first signature she can place, and a second signature the 2035 renumbering never carried forward. Mara, Tomas, Joon, and Inez each state their own terms before Elias speaks; his own part is the smallest, and he tells a **Quarter Sheet** reporter on the record that he does not know which account becomes the story. A formal objection with attached evidence is filed into the 1800 civic digest unedited. Sable offers ninety-six hours with no seal, no seizure, and a provisional classification, and it is accepted with separate custody in every domain. Inez writes down what the hold cost, including Perry Dunne’s unmoved Friday.

### Chapter 20 — Who Entered the Route
A working session starting at ten to six reads the Office’s published digest answer aloud, including *one approved outcome* backed by eleven real fatalities, and finds nobody in the room able to dismiss it. Joon reports that Nell’s signal carrier and the 05:17 route-entry carrier are the same physical path, and her latest fragment is four words: the east branch holds. The team decides against any entry, prepares a passive listening campaign with no medic on the line and a success condition of a conversation rather than an extraction, and forwards a citizens’ request for a joint structural inspection so that a refusal becomes a document. Halla Dren’s request, Joon’s refusal of it, and her warning go on the wall in that order. The envelope to Nell moves from Elias’s coat into the notebook marked not filed and not sent. A five-signature promise goes on the wall: no descent alone, four of five must agree in the same week, and the hall may stop it. The question at the end has three lines and still no name.

### Chapter 21 — The Finding
On the hold's third day **Hollis Vane** returns to the union hall with the four-page finding he wrote on Thursday, because the hold said he would write it in the room. **Finding 1**: the equipment-failure classification is not supported by the Office's own evidence, because the cause box arrives pre-ticked from the standing site pack and no field observation overwrote it before the 06:27 rescue report. **Finding 2**: the file cannot be revised in any respect depending on credential attribution, because the 4C roster has no holder field, which makes the hole the only thing a board can act on. **Finding 3** states that a named subject may withdraw the named field of a record without withdrawing the event — and **Tarin Voss** withdraws the field with his name on it, because *the body* is the word for a thing a loan gets used on. Vane records the withdrawal as a finding and not as an absence. **Niko Senn** withdraws a second page because its note says *declined publication* instead of the wording he dictated. The finding goes up on the wall with the Office's 1800 digest answer pinned directly underneath it, eleven inches apart, so the institution is on the record disagreeing with itself in one building. The team asks the board for the depot box rather than for exoneration and lodges it before the board sits. Then Inez settles the clock: the hold's last day is Saturday and the board sits Saturday afternoon, so Perry Dunne's clean week moves to the following Friday, and Perry says plainly that he did not move it to be helpful.

### Chapter 22 — A Ward With a Name On It
The hold's fourth day is ordinary labor and ordinary money. The Office truck is gone from Ash Street; **Denny Prokop** sells 240 m of good cable at 8,800 and admits in writing that *seam-rated* is his own marketing and not a rating for what the hall needs. The 1,100 dollars left in the money is a block wall, a door and a lock, and **Inez** names the wall as **a quarter** of a **4,400-dollar** requirement to make the building a building, with the other 3,300 on a list nobody would enjoy reading. **Ru Sabar** says the thing the room has been avoiding: the team has been treating the lower service run like a wound for six days, and it is the only way anything gets into the hall that will not fit through Ash Street. An Office access instrument signed by **Ilse Brauer** allows ninety minutes of non-invasive survey because Hollis Vane's Finding 2 has left the Office with no photograph of ground it is about to make a finding about. Elias wants to refuse the price of the data; **Tomas** overrules him, because a route with no paper is a route any institution can deny. The run is re-opened, laser-measured at **31.0 m**, photographed, and signed by **Emil Baros** as named keeper, resident, with **Petar Lisk** witnessing and Brauer observing; the keeper's name goes into the sealed node in free-text remarks because there is no field for it. Nine pallets of supplies go down it that afternoon. In the afternoon the **review board sits**: no seal, no clearance, the classification stays provisional, the citizens' request becomes a survey condition with an eleventh-day deadline, and the 05:02 dispatch original is ordered produced. Sable rejects Vane's Finding 4 by name as a request rather than a finding. The run is on the paper in three places, walked that day by a named person with a witness.

### Chapter 23 — The Listening Campaign
The day after the board, six hours on the bore line at the lower junction: no medic on the line, no door, no break, no opening, and Ansel Duarte's success condition read aloud by Petar Lisk. Four hours of nothing, during which Joon refuses to write *possible contact* for a relay click and records *acoustic event, unidentified, no contact* instead, and Elias is the first person to want to stop listening and the last to admit it. Nobody asks Halla Dren anything and the log says so. At 14:51 a **present-day voice** — not an echo, not a future, not a gift — says the team is the third group that has listened. It gives no name, no count and no condition, and asks for none. During contact the terminal displays a recommended re-entry and `Aftermath ahead: route will not survive another opening`, and nobody acts on it. The three wants: keep the run on the paper, send nobody in, and write nothing more than *voice at the wall*. The reason he answered is that the team published its own objection, so there was a record on his side of the concrete that could be argued with. A boundary with teeth: if one person goes into the run it shuts, because a run with a body in it is a run that frightened people close. He names **Emil Baros** and confirms he was in the alcove with the others, and says he can see the run's length, the dip at nine meters, and the door with two handles, and nothing else — he says so himself. He gives no first names, no count and no page reference, and the reason he answered at all is that the team published its own objection and gave him something to argue with.

### Chapter 24 — The Box at the Depot
The day after the board, the team uses the archive access it was granted, lawfully and awkwardly, to reach the 05:02 dispatch original in the Spine depot: box 214, out at 11:04 and back at 11:41 on two cameras, with **Hoyt** warning in front of a recorder that asking is not free and **Joon** refusing to make the request look small. The second signature on the sheet is **not a name — it is `MNT-4C-17`**, written in a box that asks for a person, and a class-three maintenance credential cannot lawfully close a two-person Continuity inspection. **Hollis Vane** draws the distinction himself: the same credential, not the same hand, and he will not let those two be confused. The **aisle log** shows box 214 opened on the day of the collapse at **14:12** under *Rout. arch. rev.* with the **subject field blank** — a field that exists, which Vane contrasts with a field that does not: a blank field is not a hole, it is a place where a name can later go without anybody lying. Vane hands over a copy because the board ordered production, and says plainly that he is not on anybody's side. While the room is still arguing about the blank subject field and before anybody leaves the archive room, **Brauer makes the Office's own copy of the aisle log page on the flatbed** — a bound single-sided ledger, one page at a time, the book never turned, the fourth column running past the edge of the glass — and initials the scan time without looking at the frame, because she is filing it and not reading it. Nine pallets of block then go down the run that afternoon at **15:00**, walked by Emil Baros and witnessed by **Anja Ro**, counted twice at 31.0 m, recorded in three places. Inez writes that the archive is now part of the problem and not only the answer.

### Chapter 25 — The Exit That Is Not There
The day after the corridor was used, the **keeper** finds it: the lower service run ends at **11.0 meters** and there is a wall. Continuous original concrete, a square-cut paint band, a length of emergency-strip conduit stopping in midair with a bright unweathered end, two meters of polished floor from a hundred and sixty boots, a scuff arc at ten meters, a floor anchor hole with a boot brace in the dust, and a right handprint at 1.40 m. The inspection niche at twelve is gone, with the survey sheet still clipped inside it. The terminal returns `no match — not in surveyed network` and a first recorded instance of 12:31 that week, because the team's own survey was the first system that ever held the run. **Tomas** stops Elias from re-measuring: you can measure the rest of it for a year and learn exactly how much is not there, which is not the same as finding out why. **Emil Baros** refuses to be a second column under the instrument, then tells the room more than the laser had, including a bar of soap he carried out of a niche in the winter of 'thirty-nine and a door he has never once been past in eleven years. **Mara** puts the honest finding on the ledger: the span that has ever carried anybody in a record coincides with the Still Hand window, the return is a wheel in a countersigned ledger, and there is no version of Saturday in which sixteen people come out without the loan — so we did not break it, we are being held to the same standard as everybody else, which is that it did not stay. The three-column page comes down into the file under a cover sheet saying it is still true about the door. **THE LOWER SERVICE RUN — WHAT IS TRUE** goes up: two columns, Saturday and Tuesday, four lines underneath, no cause, and an explicit statement that the service door is not in the table and its status is unchanged.

### Chapter 26 — The Slower Plan
Wednesday into Thursday. Elias writes a one-page plan in forty minutes with *ONE VOICE ON THE RADIO* at the bottom, and **Inez** kills it with one sentence: it is the same sentence with a different noun. She then cuts the power in front of the room and shows him that the medical bank and the lamp bank are on the same feeder, so twelve hours of "committed" load is not commitment but pressure wearing commitment's clothes. **Tomas and Mara** argue for the first time in a week — Mara will not put a medical protocol in a document because a paper read aloud above ground becomes an instruction; Tomas says a protocol you did not write is a reroute and he wrote the rule that made a man hold a frame. They settle on two refusals instead of a protocol: no medic on the line, and no predicted outcome anywhere in the document, which means the plan contains no answer to what happens if somebody is found. What comes out is four pages, none of them Elias's: four separate carded power draws a day, each revocable while running; a target list with no sequence; five domains and no coordinator; a standing refusal. **Ru Sabar** names it **THE STRAWB MAN PLAN** and withdraws her laundrette circuit permanently, and the cost is written in Inez's hand beside it — the hall dark from 0200 to 0400 every day. The count: forty-two cards in, twenty-two consent, fourteen refusal, six abstention, and every refusal read aloud.

### Chapter 27 — Friday
Perry Dunne's new Friday arrives and the crew thins before he does. The **Havenport Rescue Service recalls Rafi and Kellan** for a gas main, because the city has four certified confined-space rope technicians and three of them are in the hall, and because a west-bank pump station has been running unattended since the eleventh with two hundred people downstream — logistics, not anger, and Rafi says he should have raised it on Wednesday. **Perry Dunne** comes at eleven with a toolbox, refuses to be thanked, and says that what happened is that a hold ran to Saturday and five people signed a thing and Elias would not break it, which is not a favor and can be resented later. Joon puts two dates on the board: the survey-or-refusal deadline on the eleventh day, and the routine shuttle confirmation, due Tuesday, with the note that it is *allowed to be nothing*. The hall runs itself and runs it badly, and does it better than it used to: **Anja Ro** catches a two-minute error in Joon's custody log and makes him initial the fix, **Petar Lisk** finds the block wall four inches off square, **Ru Sabar** discovers the batteries are the wrong chemistry and have been run warm for a week, **Lena Mir** takes a full ward round with no physician in the building, and the residents rewrite the consent cards in plain sentences with no box ticked by default. Nobody wants a plan that day, and the reason is not forgetting. At nine, on the loading lip, **Ansel Duarte** tells Elias that he is not the reason the hall works, that this is not a compliment, and that the day everybody agrees with whatever Elias says is the day the building stops having a say.

### Chapter 28 — Adare Comes Back
Friday night, day thirteen, hours after the crew thinned. **R. Adare** returns with a **four**-anchor offer instead of seven — and opens by saying what the recall actually did, which is that three of the city's four rope tickets were in this hall on Thursday and that what is left is **one ticket and one safety instructor, and one ticket does not make a pair** — and admits himself that a smaller number in a tension is a worse tension and not a safer one, plus east-bank accommodation with three days of powered medical power for **Osei's wife**, whom **Lena Mir** has confirmed is on a non-powered clinic bed in a ward sharing a budget with a hall that is going dark two hours a night. The threat is not violence: a **broker license application** lodged inside the classification, and a form, three weeks out, in the hands of a neighbor, answered in a reasonable way by a reasonable person. Elias starts to refuse and **Nadi Bell** gets there first, and makes Adare write the whole offer down again in the same form as Tuesday, signs it, and explains why a man refused twice with two pieces of paper cannot tell a story about the refusal. Petar's scarecrow with a form on it goes up at the gate and stays up for a week. Six refusals, all written down by whoever made them, and none of them uses the word *anchor*. The chapter ends on the institutional question: brokers are licensed, not criminal, so this is a licensing question, and the record now shows that Sable Arden was told at ten past eleven on a Friday night, by name, in front of witnesses.

### Chapter 29 — What the Records Can Bear
Saturday. The three paper copies of Saturday's corridor survey — the original sheet, the residents' file copy, and the print out of the sealed node — disagree, and the one that disagrees is in a building the team cannot walk into. The Office's baseline form has **no keeper field**, so Emil Baros is present in free-text remarks and absent from every field query; and Brauer's single-pass scan of the bound aisle ledger **cut off the fourth column**, so the Office's copy of the log has no subject column at all and nobody in that building can see the absence. Joon writes the row: a field that does not exist, a field left blank, a field never designed for the thing in question — in all three the name is not erased, the name is not present. At 02:04 **Nell's fourth fragment arrives: *do not use the west run***, on the same present-day branch-crossed carrier, which runs through twenty meters that are no longer there; Joon records the carrier fact and refuses to interpret it. Elias wants to file the unsent envelope. **Joon refuses him out loud in front of witnesses** — filing makes it a record, and a record gets answered by a building. **Mara refuses him coldly** — filing a present voice makes it a search, and a search is a promise made to somebody behind a wall. Elias does not file, gives the honest reason (the only line that could carry a reply is the one she has just told him not to use), and adds that the real reason is that he is afraid of a survey. The envelope stays in the notebook, and the board carries the question nobody can answer: is the old maintenance route a way to reach her, or a way to close the branch she is keeping open?

### Chapter 30 — The Ward Holds a Shift
Sunday is a working shift, not a ceremony, and its structure is five refusals. Mara: no medic on the line, no predicted outcome, nobody moves a person for their own benefit. Tomas: no door, and his number is written before anybody goes and he does not argue with the instrument, and four other people may now say *enough*. Joon: no record in one building, nothing consolidated, nothing published that a person has not permitted. Inez: no load without a card, no unannounced blackouts, and the right to cut. Elias adds a clause nobody asked for: *I am not going to be the last person to speak on any of this, and the last word does not close the item.* **The site is held**, with the cost written in Inez's hand beside the gain: the joint-inspection condition lapses, the standing designation is unchallenged for a cycle, the 2031 node is not examined, the sealed node becomes the only version of that ground, and the man behind the wall has no way in. What they prepare instead is a method with no date on it. Elias signs a promise **Ansel Duarte is empowered to break** by saying *that is a rescue*, with no reason required and no requirement to be right, and Ansel insists the inconvenient clause stays in. The closing discovery: the copy of the possible-Elias audio in the sealed node is **0.4 seconds longer** than the team's, with a flat carrier gap in the middle, and Joon records the fact and refuses, out loud in the room, to put a name or a meaning under it. The board's last line is the next action — Tuesday's refusal, a rescanned ledger with all four columns, and a Monday descent — the board leads with Monday, not Sunday, because Chapter 30 *is* Sunday — to ask a man behind a wall what he wants to do about a road that is now eleven meters long, and then do what he says.


### Chapter 31 — What He Wants
Monday, day sixteen, the descent the Chapter 30 board promised. The argument is not about whether to go down but about who has signed: four of five, in the same week, and this week started yesterday, so Inez refuses a four o'clock departure and sends them at one. Mara signs under her two standing conditions and adds a third refusal for today — if he offers a route, a name, or a way out, Elias does not say yes in the next ten minutes. Tomas signs his number (0.4 mm at the lip) and notes that **Doss comes as far as the fence and does not go down, because one rope ticket and one safety instructor is not a pair and today is possible only because nobody is going in**. Joon signs and repeats his refusal of a meaning for the 0.4 seconds in advance, on the record, before they leave. At the loading lip, Emil Baros reports **eleven again, four times this week, not moving**, and writes it as an observer; the bar of soap from the missing niche is in his coat pocket. Elias kneels at the eleven-meter mark with a card Petar wrote, tells the man the truth in numbers rather than in weather, and is corrected twice on the wire — *in use*, not *road*; a line is live when he picks up a handset. Elias throws away nine of eleven questions and asks the smallest one, *when did it change for you*, and the answer comes in his units: Saturday he could turn a loaded cart in the dip; on Tuesday he could not, and he found out with a dolly and a half load of rice. **He gives one fact for free — that he came through on the collapse Saturday — and refuses the other, and says the refusal is the point.** His three wants, none of them a rescue and none of them a favor: the eleven meters on the paper as eleven meters with thirty-one and the dates and his own words; the days he has said he will be there on the board, explicitly not as appointments; and **nobody runs up the ramp to tell him the world got smaller**, which he calls procedure and not mercy, because he has had mercy from people who were storing it. Elias puts the card away instead of reading it, and Ru Sabar says at the fence that when Inez makes him put this in the power column he is going to try to give the man a card. The board gets four new lines and a fifth Petar wrote from something Emil Baros said: **he has a stop on all of it, like the rest of us, and he is not on the board.**

### Chapter 32 — Tuesday
Day seventeen, and the eleventh day, and Joon writes both numbers on the board side by side with a line saying they are not the same number and neither one is the other one. **The written statement of refusal goes in at nine in Inez's hand, on the back of the citizens' request, with four reasons and none of them brave** — no trained structural person and no rope pair; going to look at the pour means going along a run and that is a rescue; a woman nineteen years ahead told us a named place becomes a place to be closed and we refused her once and may not ask again; we would rather have a document that says why we did not ask. **The joint-inspection condition lapses at ten past nine.** At the Spine, **Ilse Brauer rescan is done properly** — the ledger is four columns wide because a 2031 office dropped a fifth field out of the print, so the subject column was always in the book and half an inch past the platen; she photographs in two frames and stitches them by hand. **The fourth column comes back and the 14:12 subject field is still empty, and for the first time since the collapse somebody in that building can see the blank.** Joon says it out loud before the frame exists, with Vane present. **Vane: a rescanned page is not the same as a page nobody looked at.** Brauer: *I have made this document worse and I made it true.* At the transit operations desk, **the shuttle-six confirmation comes out on day seventeen rather than the nine Bex Amadi promised on day three, because a freeze on any query touching the incident is a freeze on the incident's own paperwork.** It is a count, not a manifest: the early run four, **the mid run five**, and the official building count is four. The fifth person existed and moved that morning and is in a record as a number. **Nadi Bell refuses to let Joon push** — the freeze protects the Office's own queue, and every day it is on is a day the thing that removed her sister is in charge of the paperwork about it. Her gain: the count is now five, dated, in four buildings, and in six weeks somebody will have a reason to fill a visible field. The board ends on a four-year-old's line: **they have found out you can see a blank now, and we do not know how long that takes.**

### Chapter 33 — The License
Tuesday night, day seventeen. Sable Arden comes to the hall with a courier and answers Vane's four findings in writing as the Director rather than as the chair. **Finding 1 accepted with a mechanism** — a provisional classification can no longer carry a cause no observer entered, and it will take nine weeks to clear the desks. **Finding 2 done**: sector 4C class three, early sequence, **nine credentials and nine holder fields not carried forward** — the mapping was consolidated in 2039 into a Cordell regional archive the Office does not control, so the answer moved one building over, and she refuses to present that as a result. **Finding 3 adopted as office practice**, with the cost stated: five years of forms with a withdrawal line and a third of her clerks getting it wrong for a year. **Finding 4 rejected by name**, with the real reason, and with something worse: **a provisional classification may not stand ninety days without a seal or a finding**, so four days of refusing an inspection has set a timer on the thing Elias fears, and she says he is making a mistake for a good reason. **R. Adare is licensed**, effective Tuesday, inside the Kestrel provisional classification — he had the routing log and the continuity chain by Saturday morning and did not declare them, and Sable approved it because the log is the best record of that ground anybody will have. Conditions: consent, witness and return recorded; a log with time, place and broker of record; **notification to the residents' body within seventy-two hours**; an unannounced Office observer entitled to attend. **She names the coercion herself: a clause that can be broken by a clock is a clause that says tell them afterwards, if you can.** Inez and Petar find that the observer need not come from outside. Nadi Bell makes Sable say what she actually wants: **the hall's number as the population of record**, in exchange for a standing published observer on the Quarter's street. Sable refuses to take the offer back and admits it is pressure. The board's last line is Nadi's: **a license is a promise that a bad thing will at least be written down, she wants one, that is not enough.**

### Chapter 34 — The Offer
Wednesday, day eighteen. Sable's second letter arrives on its own, in a plain envelope, no courier, exactly as promised, and says plainly that the re-entry is not a seizure: we have two hundred and ten meters of migrating void under an apron people walk over, and when it eventually happens the question in every inquiry will be why nobody looked, and the answer will be *because the neighborhood preferred not to have anybody look*, and that answer will be true because of a form the hall filed with her signature on it. **The Office offers an entry with two named confined-space technicians from the regional pool** — a pair, which the hall has not had since day thirteen — plus Office instruments, a chain, a medical standard written by Mara that is a present standard, and the hall's own resident observer with the right to stop. **Doss does the arithmetic and is ashamed of the problem he cannot solve himself.** **Joon counts what is on the table: three things for one, and the three are not the price of the entry, they are the price of the entry being ours** — the notebook, the raw audio including the eleven seconds, and the observer's read line on the sealed node, which is the only window the hall has ever had into what the Office holds about its own ground. **The team counters.** The chain needs a name, the only structural person in the building put his own nine-line disclosure of the 2034 same-relay finding and the 2029 panel into the file before the entry, in his own words, at Mara's insistence, and Petar read it out twice. **Sable accepts in substance and refuses one line: the Office cannot be bound by a residents' body's decisions**, and she attaches the five-name promise herself as a condition the Office will honor. **Elias says *good* out loud and on the first try and asks for it minuted.** Then Inez, who demanded the routing log at twenty to nine using the license's own clause, gets a form with a box for a number and three words about consent and **no box for a street** — two events, one subject at 21:50 Tuesday and **three subjects at 04:10 this morning**, while the hall was reading a letter about a rope pair. Petar's line: **somebody decided that on purpose, and the somebody was a form designer in an office, and he never met any of us.**

### Chapter 35 — The Circuit
Thursday, day nineteen. **Bess Ferran**, thirty-one, a night-shift phlebotomist, was routed at 04:10 on Wednesday and walked to the hall on Thursday morning, carrying a paper coat and a card nobody gave her, and holding a paper somebody wrote *Ash Street* on in her own handwriting at the door. She came back not because she came back but because of that paper, and nobody knows whose idea it was. **The room on Calder Street holds nine people standing in a closed ring two meters across with their feet on a line of tape**, told they are waiting, with a man in the middle with a clipboard who calls the number *the pair* and a woman who is very kind. The room got easier when a man put a hand on the outside and said *three*. Two loads, or one load with a bridge, and either way it is a number and the nine are not told which. The team will not go in: **Elias catches himself nine words into a plan and says out loud that that is a rescue and that he is not going to make Ansel break it, and Ansel chooses not to break it and says so on the record so that in three weeks Elias will have the other version in his head first — break the clause the day somebody is in a hole, do not waste it on a room with a kettle in it.** They go across at half past eleven, stand on the pavement in the rain, and Elias says it from the bottom of the stairs as information and never once says *get out*, and answers *what would you do instead* with *I do not know, and I am not going to invent one for a person standing in a ring at midnight*, and names the warm room on Ash Street without calling it an offer. Two men come down, one of them Gil Trejo, fifty-four, with a bus ticket. **Wendell Pryce, fifty-eight, a retired signalman, comes down to say he has been in one of these in Cordell in thirty-six, knows what the ring is, and would like to keep paying ninety dollars for it, and is not asking permission but telling them so nobody can write later that nobody was asked.** The load drops at twenty past one and **Teodor**, mid-route on a crossing he bought with his own name for his mother Marija, comes back on his hands and knees and says, to nobody: *I was on it and I could see the other side, and then it went out like a lamp. I want somebody to tell me what that was.* Nobody asks him to be grateful. The board line: **they are not crooks, they are cheaper than we are and safer than we are, and both of those are true, and we have been losing that argument since Friday night and losing it wrong.**

### Chapter 36 — The Cost of the Good Offer
Friday, day twenty. The entry runs at **eight in the morning, not first light, because Teodor said on Thursday night that first light is when things happen to people on their way to work and Inez put it on the board**, and Sable's office agreed in forty minutes without ever saying no — the only evidence all week that a condition works. Two named technicians, **Ruzicka and Ohno**, an entry limit written by Tomas and accepted without a word. The 2031 pour is where the survey said and terminates six meters short of the camera target, and **at thirty-eight centimeters there is a second pour stamped 2038 with no drawing in any of the four buildings and a hairline crack a meter and a half long**, photographed with a scale, and nothing touches it. Then three things fail. **Inez gave the clinic's generator margin to the entry instruments and Comfort Osei's frame went dark at 09:20**, and she says so first, and that she would do it again, and that the records room goes on a lamp for a day, which is worse than money because money comes back. **Ru Sabar offers her machine-shop card back and Inez cannot take it**, because a withdrawal that can be resumed was not a withdrawal, and Ru says: *you have been telling me for six days that a card is a card, and now my card is a card that only works one way.* **Mara makes a triage call out loud, in the open, with Osei eight feet away**: one powered hour, Ansel's sacral lesion against Comfort Osei's back, and three statements, the third being that she would still have given the generator away at seven in the morning because a seam over a school is worse than a back. Osei says *give it to the boy.* **Tomas's name went into a record he did not want it in**, cross-indexed from the 2034 write-off to the chain that places him under the apron, with an Office form asking him to confirm whether he will be available to give evidence at any future inquiry; he admits he told the room about the collapse and not that he signed off the node the collapse is about. Joon refuses to let anybody be comforted: **we made him do this.** At the gate, **Teodor becomes the sixth name on the stop list without a card**, and gives the reason: somebody has to, and it should be somebody who does not have to be here. His sentence goes on the wall and stays: **nobody asked me if I wanted to be a door.**

### Chapter 37 — Saturday
Saturday, day twenty-one. Care and labor: Lena Mir's ward round in fifty-one minutes with two nurse-assistants and no physician; Oren Pike and Anja Ro walking to the end of the hall; Ruta Amsel with the brace off for the first time in nine days; **Emil Baros writing *keeper present, nothing changed since Monday* for the ninth day** because the keeper's line is eleven meters away and he cannot reach it; the dark hours from two with Petar and a volunteer. **Perry Dunne asks again for a week with none of the crew on the street**, says the clean week was never delivered and it is nobody's fault, warns that the building has rearranged itself around not needing the two men the Service took, says the regional pool arriving is the best news he has had and he does not trust it, and says he will not ask twice. The Office's daily return demands a **count of persons present** and will accept nothing else. **Nadi Bell refuses in her own name** — a count cannot lose anybody, a name can, and her mother's sentence about a hospital list in nineteen forty-nine is still true — and substitutes a **movement record**: dated, one line per person, in, out, or here, where to, by what ordinary road, in the person's own words, and nothing leaves the building without permission. She says the second reason she likes it is that the Office will not accept it, so two versions of the truth sit in two buildings. **Niko Senn chooses to leave for his sister's on the east bank, on a bus, on an ordinary road**, not because he is better but because every conversation in the hall is about a road that used to be there and he wants one decision that is his and does not go in a file. **The paper count moves from twenty-three to twenty-two and Joon updates the record at ten past five with Niko standing there holding his coat.** The board's second line: **we are twenty-two. We are not a number. Here is who is here, who is going, where they are going, and who said so.**

### Chapter 38 — Saturday Night
Saturday night, day twenty-one, in the records room on a lamp because Friday took the clinic's generator margin. Joon plays four seconds of room tone, then the team's copy, then the node's copy. **The eleven seconds are byte-identical in both copies and the room tone is the same room; the file is four tenths of a second longer and the four tenths are in the middle and they are not silence.** The temptation in the room is named twice: **Mara, who would like it very much to be a machine**, and **Elias, who asks for the terminal at one in the morning and is refused by a Joon who explains that a prefix in a log is not a gap, it is a record of a man wanting to know, and that is how a file becomes a story.** **Petar Lisk is right and nobody saw it coming: *you told us not to say what it means, so we will not say what it means. Can we still write down the number?*** The number is a Civic Spine relay stamp — **`K-7Q` and four digits** — in a format **not in the Havenport stamp set indexed to the file, seen once before, not placed.** It is a fact and it is not usable on Saturday night by six people in a closet, and Joon writes down that he has seen that format once before and is not going to put the two next to each other tonight. Elias states that he has no intention of asking his own future for anything, and Joon writes that the sentence means he is not the person who asks, which is the whole difference. The board's last line is Petar's: **the voice is still unknown, the copy is four tenths longer, we have one number and it is not a place, and on Monday we will be bored about it, which is what I have been told to write and not what I think.**

### Chapter 39 — The Old Relay
Sunday, day twenty-two. Joon has a forty-one-second carry with nothing on it at four in the afternoon, and admits he missed a second thing at 02:10 that morning and tells the room he missed it. Both come on the 4C local route line, the same physical path as the 05:17 maintenance entry, and the second one comes up on **a licensed broker's branch circuit with a broker of record who was asleep.** A carry is a direction, not a place. **Elias builds a three-stage retrieval plan before dinner and Nadi Bell puts her hand on it and makes him say the word**; Mara refuses to let a future self authorize a present medical decision and says the rule goes both ways; Tomas seconds a medical refusal and asks that it be minuted; **Ansel offers his sentence and Elias uses it on himself.** At 22:14 the notice comes up on the broker's circuit: **Loan offered: Nell Rook, nineteen years ahead. Gift name: Door Memory. Gift: I know a way out of here, I have walked it, it is there this minute. Boundary: I will not open it for one person and let it close on the rest. Return: when you have written down that you did not use it.** **Joon refuses to be the man who says do not answer her**, because he has spent a fortnight refusing to speak for people on the other side of that wall. **Elias names the cost of the envelope out loud: a message from him to his sister costs a night of somebody's feet in a ring on Calder Street**, and says the reason has changed and he will not pretend the old reason was the better one. She speaks twice more. The **want**: keep the exit on her side open, not for her, for the people standing near it and the ones no list has. The **boundary**: if he comes through it comes through; the door is held by people because nobody has made it official, and the moment it is official it closes for them and not for him; **do not make my road into an Office document.** The **reason she answered**: *you published your own objection.* The clue, brief: it is not the door they have been walking; it is past the wall the corridor ends at and **under the water**, because the water is the only thing nobody on her side is allowed to touch; and when he gets near it, do not count. **Joon works out that the man behind the wall has been standing on top of the relay for twenty-two days and has never mentioned it, and is not going to ask him, because he said he would answer one thing and he has not been asked.**

### Chapter 40 — The Route That Is Not Home
Monday, day twenty-three. The notice is still on the terminal. **Mara argues for using the road**, out loud, about the unconfirmed second reading at nine meters, and will not soften or withdraw it, and then argues against using a future person to authorize a present decision, because the rule that no present person may be required to authorize a future self's capacity goes both ways and the both ways is the only part that means anything. Nadi Bell puts her own reason on the table — her sister is one of the invisible — so that nobody can use it later without admitting they used it. **Elias stands up and says *that is a rescue*, about himself, first, before anybody has finished arguing**, and then says the five things that have to be written down separately because four are the same and one is different. **Ansel: second time, and I am keeping count of a thing that is mine.** The refusal goes on the page in full — no loan offered, no capacity taken, no boundary stretched, nothing signed by anybody who was not asked, the gift not accepted as a loan and the route not accepted as a route — and **Joon's distinction goes with it: the return is not invoked, it is performed**, and in ten years somebody will read *the return was satisfied* and conclude a deal was done. **Niko Senn's Chapter 21 precedent holds from an ordinary bus: say *declined*, not *not used*.** Elias keeps the Monday appointment, says the length has not changed, admits he did not come Thursday and did not say so because that is what was agreed, and tells the man at the wall that there is a road he is not taking, because he is the only person Elias owes that sentence to. The man says it is a waste, then gives away one thing and puts the handset down mid-sentence: *it is warm on that side, in February, under the water.* Elias does not move, does not go and look for the man, and goes home. The board names the volume's next movement — a thermal survey on Wednesday for the reading at nine meters, and then the relay, with two reasons named separately and a rule about which one they are allowed to use — and ends with the three live questions: who held `MNT-4C-17` and who signed the second line, whether the relay is a way to reach her or the thing that closes her side, and what the Office believes a single approved outcome prevents.


### Chapter 41 — Tuesday, the Day After
Tuesday, day twenty-four, the day after the refusal, in a hall that has been running for a month. **Lena Mir gets a sentence in the plan in her own handwriting** — the medical decisions do not stop, they get done by the person who is not writing your plan down — because if it goes wrong the person the residents ask is not going to be Elias. **Three fronts on the board by nine**: the ward's four carded draws and two dark hours and Comfort Osei's frame; the Office's ninety-day clock, which Petar Lisk now writes up every morning as **eighty-three days, counted from the seventeenth**; and Calder Street, where the license has been used five times for ten subjects with consent, witness and return on file and **no observer at either event, because the clause says *may***. Doss needs two names on a piece of paper, not a promise. **Sable assigns Ruzicka and Ohno for Wednesday under four conditions, and the second one is the important one: the survey stops at the stated time and nobody on this site can extend it, including this office.** Inez takes the first three and **writes a fifth in her own column: instrument power is a residents' carded draw that any household may cut with no notice.** The cost is named before it is incurred and it lands on Ru Sabar, whose card is still in the envelope, and on two households on Bell Row. Mara's medical standard **has no contingency section and she has written *no contingency* across it** so a clerk in a hurry does not help her. Tomas's number goes above the technicians' on the survey sheet, and **Petar Lisk's limit is the one that survives: we are going to look at a number, the number has been on a sheet since the fourth day, and we are not going to decide anything about it before we see it.** Joon closes the chapter with the question the batch is made of — what is the survey for, them or the file — and with the fact nobody had checked: **the first person in that building to say out loud that a document is for the file and not for the people in it was Petar Lisk, on the seventh night, about people he lives with.**

### Chapter 42 — The Survey
Wednesday, day twenty-five, the survey already on the board. First light, four degrees colder. Ohno reads the chain twice; the residents' copy is in a plastic wallet Ru Sabar carried down herself. **Petar Lisk reads the limits; Emil Baros writes *ELEVEN METERS. NOT MOVING* for the fourteenth day; Teodor is the residents' observer on the chain, which he did not want, and Ruzicka says "Good."** At four and a half meters the gas tells them what it is: **the CO2 curve has been flat for seventeen days, and a room with a person in it does not do that — it is a room with something in it replacing the air.** At 9.2 m there is **a brick lining across the run that is on no drawing in four buildings**, and behind it a brick chamber nine meters long, four wide, curved ceiling, four degrees warmer. Ruzicka's count: **eight discrete heat sources**; her signed figure is **four confirmed, one probable, one unknown — six** — and the difference between her six and the machine's eight is a stove and a man who will not lie still. Eight sources, seven people. **The only source the camera can see moving is a man sitting up against the far end**, and he is the seventh person, and the machine's eighth source is a rectangle the size of a small stove. Teodor says the word *stop* over the auger on the 2038 pour, with no reason, and Ruzicka puts the case down herself. **Sable's second condition comes at eight o'clock and the survey stops**; Joon writes *they did not push* in the same hand as the number. Mara makes three decisions in public and writes no survival window, no protocol, and no triage in advance. **The number nobody wanted is four confirmed, one probable, one unknown, in a chamber that has been under a street for twenty-five days with air that is being replaced.**

### Chapter 43 — The Argument About the Number
Thursday, day twenty-six. The Office's return grew a new middle line overnight — **persons recovered from site** — with a revision letter, and Joon records *revision in the middle of the form* without interpreting it. **Nadi Bell says in the first minute that her refusal does not cover people the hall has never met**, and then extends it anyway, on her own logic, out loud, before anybody agrees: *a count is a number of bodies and a movement record is a set of sentences about people, and the difference is the tense.* She writes a line for people the hall has never met, in a past tense, in advance, on her own authority. Joon says the cost out loud first — the line will be the answer to *why did nobody look* — and Nadi says write it anyway. **Joon's Group D beat: the only name those seven people have is a letter a clerk put on our own sheet on the fourth day, and the difference between the machine's eight and the technician's six is a stove and a man who will not lie still, and Joon's last line is that a stove in a record is a thing that later becomes a person.** Mara fills the middle line with a sentence rather than a number and refuses the survival-window box in words a clerk cannot misfile as a finding. **Petar Lisk writes the difference between the two: a plan has a time on it, a rescue has a person on it.** Five domains each say their own piece, and the specific obstacle is six words: **the wall is the only way in.**

### Chapter 44 — Split Authority
Friday, day twenty-seven. **Ru Sabar's card ran from seven in the morning and out at six, and the corridor repeater goes with it.** **Perry Dunne gives away the week he asked for twice** and asks not to be told who gets it. **Emil Baros draws the map** — to scale, from the second day's measurements, with the dip, the water, and the brick lining at 9.2, and **no absent thing on it**, which is the first time in a month a document in that building managed that. Elias draws a line through the lining and says somebody is going to stop him and nobody does. **Inez stops him, and she is right and he is wrong**: he has built a night operation around a card that expires at six and has never built a plan around the hall's own dark hours, and he lives there. *If your operation runs through two o'clock then this hall goes dark at two — this hall, the four households on the card, and a nineteen-year-old on a powered frame who cannot turn himself.* **Mara challenges the word retrieval**: retrieval is a word about objects, and if that goes in a document then in four years there will be a paper that says the retrieval failed and will not say whose fault, and the fault will be arithmetic. **Elias says the sentence no plan in this room has ever contained: there is no version of this week where everybody comes out and nobody stays in it, and anybody who tells you there is one is selling you a line on a map.** The plan goes on the wall in Baros's hand, with a fresh expiry — *0600 Sunday, or the fourth person, whichever is earlier, and it does not carry over* — and a fresh six-line stop page with Teodor's name on it.

### Chapter 45 — Saturday
Saturday, day twenty-eight, care and the ordinary labor of a building that is about to become a route. Lena's round in forty-nine minutes. Comfort Osei is at the clinic on a bed and not on a frame, and **Inez tells Osei Kwarteng to his face that the east ward is dark two hours a night and that she would not put his wife on a frame that goes off for two hours**, and he says *I gave it away too.* **Ansel Duarte says one word — *No*** — to the plan's route through the residents' sleeping room, on the ground that a stranger in a corridor at two in the morning is a promise about a place, and it costs eleven meters and a wet floor, and Inez pays. **Nadi Bell asks the twenty-two how long they think they will be here**, and the answer is that the building is now a ward maintained by the people in it, and she names the cost: it costs the count, and the Office will write a reasonable paragraph, and Elias tells her to write it herself first. **Emil Baros asks what he is going to be, in writing, next to the twenty-two, and wants somebody else to have to say yes out loud**; the room says yes one name at a time, and Lena Mir says the next keeper's line has to be somebody who can walk it.

### Chapter 46 — The Boundary Holds
Sunday, day twenty-nine. The brick comes down at ten past six. **Doss has a rope pair for the first time since the thirteenth** — himself plus Ruzicka and Ohno, who came back on their own time. Teodor holds the stop page; Tomas stands at the mouth and will not go down. At 16:08 a **Still Hand arrives from Mara Okafor twenty-one years ahead** — not seventeen, so not the first one — boundary *one body*, return *when the fourth person is through the wall*, a return Joon notes was **already satisfied four hours earlier**. **Elias refuses it out loud, first, in front of everybody**, then says in the room, in front of witnesses, that he is going to ask whether the boundary holds for a second body, and why, in advance — and Nadi makes him record that he could have said no and did not. The answer is *one body* and *no answer*. **Joon writes the distinction that is the batch: the refusal is the record and the question is also the record; no boundary was stretched because stretching it would have required asking, and the asking is what ended it; the return was not invoked because there was nothing to invoke.** Elias says the sentence and asks for it in two voices: **this is not a malfunction — a refusal is not a fault in a machine, it is the form the thing takes when it is a person.** **The fourth person comes through at 12:20 and dies at 13:10 in the hall with a blanket on him**, and Mara says it in front of the room. **The plan has two triggers and the first one fired at 05:50, ten minutes before the brick came down, with nobody watching the clock; the second fired at 12:20 with this crossing and is the one everybody remembers. Petar Lisk reads the board at 12:00 and again at 13:10 and writes four lines under the times: *PLAN EXPIRED 0600. NOTICED 1310. WE ARE NOT STOPPING. SOMEBODY HAS TO WRITE A NEW ONE AND NONE OF US IS GOING TO.*** The plan is dead on the page from 05:50 and nobody writes a second one. **Chapter 46 carries one interface notice only; the withdrawal is rendered in prose as four fields, the third of them empty.** Then rope, shoring, thermal imaging, a board and four hands, and eleven hours. **Mara gives the cost once, in the corridor at 21:20: the first time a future person withdrew instead of giving, it was because of something we did.** She does not speak to Elias again that night, and her last line of the chapter is that the fifth goes as soon as the regional pair is on the street, and will not be tomorrow morning.

### Chapter 47 — Monday
Monday, day thirty, the slow route and what it asks of ordinary bodies. The regional pool is four hours late with the pair and Doss says the correct answer and then says he cannot go past nine and a half until eleven. **Inez prices the twenty minutes of Doss off the board as four minutes a person and gives it anyway.** At ten past four, mid-operation, **Elias feeds a handset eleven meters down the corridor because a promise that is only kept when it is convenient is a schedule and not a promise.** What he says is short and is not about the road: six people in the chamber, five out, one to come, one died; and on Tuesday the corridor is a work site, eleven meters, and nobody is coming to get him. **The man gives one true thing back: it is not warm today, and it has not been since Monday, and he noticed at eleven o'clock in the morning.** Elias does not ask the question, which has been on the end of his tongue since the ninth day, and the reason for the promise is not that the man might say no — **the reason is Halla Dren on the fourteenth day and a shaft they were not to name and a woman they refused once and are not permitted to ask twice.** Joon refuses a meaning out loud in daylight and says he is saying so rather than just not writing, because silence looks exactly like a decision. **The fifth person comes out at twenty past eight, fifty-one pounds, and says his own name twice.** He is the man who was sitting up at the far end. **The chapter closes on the fact that the plan expired at 05:50 on Sunday, ten minutes before the wall was opened, that nobody noticed until 13:10, and that three more days of work have been done with nothing on the wall behind it.**

### Chapter 48 — The Approved Sequence
Tuesday, day thirty-one. At ten past six the terminal produces something with no gift name on it and two headings the hall has refused before: **`Recommended re-entry`**, and a gift field offering to make the relay take one order, with the boundary **the order is obeyed, not answered, nobody in your hall is asked**, and a return that is the branch-side relay four hours from now **and a message Elias can put in her hand himself**. Joon says it is the same voice as the eleven seconds and refuses to interpret it; Petar notices the eleven seconds said *the map is not to be made to agree* and this says *nobody in your hall is asked*. **Inez names it in one sentence: you are not being offered a hand, you are being offered my board with me not asked.** Five people say their own piece honestly: Mara argues for it and admits she most wants to say no; **Tomas says he would sign it and asks to be overheard**; Doss says he could not give one reason why he would not; Joon says the horror is that the file would be accurate; Nadi Bell has already written the line. **Elias refuses, and says the second reason out loud: it is the only thing anybody has offered him this month that gets him his sister, and the order those two things happened in is the whole of what he learned this month.** **Ansel's sentence is available and he does not need it, and Petar Lisk writes down what he did not say.** The refusal costs the four-C local route and all four fragments of Nell's present-day carry, and Petar Lisk initialed the time because Joon asked for somebody who was not him. The second entry goes in at one, and **Ivy Ambrose, seven, comes out at nine minutes past six.** The second person does not come out that night.

### Chapter 49 — The Living Route
Wednesday, day thirty-two. **The chapter opens in the present tense with the seventh attempt.** At 06:10 the crew goes down with no plan behind them; Ruzicka's camera finds Perpetua Oyelaran standing and holding the wall with her left hand and unable to take a step without it; the medical answer is that a board put under her in the chamber chooses which way she goes before she chooses anything; **Doss says the thing out loud and it goes in Lena Mir's page and not into a corridor, and she is not attempted further.** The ward runs on residents: Lena's round in fifty-two minutes for twenty-eight people, Anja Ro on a board, Ru Sabar six hours on a wet floor with a light, Oren Pike on the corridor line, Nadi Bell's curtain between the Ambrose sisters, power from two households on Bell Row. **Tomas does his arithmetic in nine lines on the wall and refuses to move the shoring**, on a number of his own — *anything that moves at the mouth of this hole moves toward the apron* — and asks for it in his own hand and not a summary, and says the ninth line himself: **there is one person left in that chamber and I am not going to get her — and the plan did expire, at ten minutes to six on Sunday morning, and the plan did not stop anything, and what stopped was the drawing of another one, and the reason there is not another one is that he would not draw it and nobody else would draw it over him.** Inez then writes the closure on the board: the operation ended at ten minutes to six on Sunday, ten minutes before it began, ran three days with nothing on the wall behind it, and a seal goes on with a woman in that chamber, and her name goes on the wall in Inez's hand. **Perpetua Oyelaran, thirty-four, stays under Ash Street.** The signal returns at 16:20, twenty-seven hours and thirty-nine minutes after it went out, with a `Warning: the branch is changing` and **eleven words: *I am not the road. I am the person who lives beside it. Do not count us.***, on a four-tenths of a second relay stamp **seen for the second time** with no meaning attached, and Joon writes that it is the second time and refuses what it means. **Elias refuses to travel toward a relay because a woman whose road he has already refused is standing near one.** The board closes with a list of things the team will not do and Petar's line: **we have one person under the street and we know her name.** Nadi writes that the hall is twenty-two and also twenty-eight and only one of those is a count.

### Chapter 50 — The Sealed Tunnel
Thursday, day thirty-three, the volume close. The Spine seals Kestrel and classifies the collapse as *catastrophic progressive structural failure attributable to legacy installation condition*, which is **nine words** that mean *equipment failed*. **Sable Arden signs it and is right on the evidence she holds.** Hollis Vane enters it by hand into a file that holds his own Finding 1, **accepted sixteen days earlier on the seventeenth**, and does not remark on it; he separates the count of the persons the site produced from the count of the people who were in the ground **without naming a number, because no number in that file carries both**; Nadi says *do not let them be consolidated*; Vane says that is a job description. Eleven boxes leave on a handcart, and a copy of the record had already gone out of the city on a bus on the Sunday morning of the twenty-ninth. **At four o'clock Elias tells the man behind the wall that the corridor is being sealed and that he is inside the thing being closed, and the man says *do not write me down*, and Elias does not argue.** The hall records the refusal, the reason, the date and no name; the man says it is not warm on his side either, that he will not be there on a Thursday after this one, and that eleven meters is a length and not a number. **Elias and Mara's working rule: any offer, boundary, refusal or condition goes to the people who pay for it, in the words it was said in, before it is acted on — and nobody inherits her.** Nothing has happened between them and no future version of either of them has promised them anything. Elias writes the notebook and **gives up the claim that a good rescue means he brings her back next**, and beneath the signal he writes a rescued passenger's name in ballpoint that the Civic Spine terminal cannot find. **At eleven o'clock the wall gives one last echo and a voice that sounds like his asks him to promise that he will not force a future person to answer when that future person says no, and Elias does not make the promise** — not because it is wrong, but because making it would be forcing a version of him that had not been asked. *He did not make the promise. He also did not say no.* Petar Lisk writes it at his request and nobody else stood up. The broker's license named a sealed site and nobody told the broker. The envelope is still unsent and the reason is four words: *the road is sealed.* The volume closes on Petar Lisk's board at seven on Friday morning: *who edited the Kestrel response record so that a living passenger and a woman's route were both called administrative errors — and what does the Continuity Office believe that a single approved outcome would prevent?*

---

# Volume 02 Chapter Summaries — Batch 0001 (Chapters 51–60)

**Day zero is Saturday the sixth of September 2042.** All dates below are derived from that and every weekday was checked against `outline/volume-02.md`.

### Chapter 51 — The Notice
Friday, day thirty-four. Petar Lisk reads the board at seven and reads the Chapter 50 question twice, both halves, with a bracket under the second one that nobody has answered. **Emil Baros has walked to the loading lip for fourteen mornings and is turned back at a fence, and nobody has asked him a question.** The Deputy Director, **Nathan Rusk**, arrives at twenty to nine, does not sit down for eleven minutes, reads the whole wall, and stops twice — at Tomas's nine lines and at the promise that Elias is not the person who asks his own future for anything. **Two instruments in one envelope, deliberately out of order**, with the reason printed on the front: *the second instrument does not require anybody to be right.* The **Notice of Inquiry, Kestrel Response 2042**, case number **HC-42-0417**, three sittings, **five questions**; and the **Order of Impoundment**, one page, no finding, no hearing, dated the day it was served. Rusk's position in plain words: *I am not asking whether you broke a rule. I am asking whether the thing you did made the ground do that.* He volunteers that Sable Arden signed the seal and the classification, that both stand, and that he cannot move them. **Elias asks for the five questions in writing before anybody answers any of them**, reads them aloud, and the fifth is *who altered the Kestrel response record*. He says the one true thing available — nine words, a description and not a cause, and you cannot get a person out of a sentence with no person in it — and Rusk writes it down because he said he would. **Elias promises nothing, says so once in a corridor with no witness, and the room lets it go.** The corridor sentence is *"I do not know what to do this morning"* — he has not written one thing down since half past seven, and he has **never once said that sentence about a Friday** — **and the chapter fixes its own return in the same paragraph: he did not say it again for eleven days. Day 34 plus eleven is day 45, which is Chapter 62 and the Cordell visit.** A later review pass found this plant in no handoff file at all and put it in `state/open-threads.md` as item 41 and on the Batch 0002 Chapter 62 card. Petar puts the case number under the question: **HC-42-0417. THEY GAVE US A NUMBER FOR THE QUESTION. THAT IS THE FIRST TIME SOMEBODY HAS GIVEN US A NUMBER FOR ANYTHING.**

### Chapter 52 — What the Office Holds
Saturday, day thirty-five, the Spine depot, aisle nine, because the public counter and the aisle run Saturdays and Ilse Brauer works Saturdays and not Sundays and the aisle locks at six. Hollis Vane says in the first minute that he has not chosen to be the custodian of the inquiry's exhibits. **The Office holds the offer, the boundary, the gift, the return, the countersignature and no name**, because Tarin Voss withdrew the field naming him as the body on the sixteenth and the withdrawal is Finding 3 — **and the Office holds no record of the question Elias asked in front of witnesses on the twenty-ninth day**, because it is in Joon's hand and in the residents' file that is about to be the subject of a demand. Vane says so two days early on purpose. **The 14:12 subject field is filled in**, by Brauer, at 11:12, standing up, with a pen, in the room, eighteen days after her own hand restored the column, and she asks afterwards whether she is permitted to. **Vane's sentence is the volume's institutional thesis and is said once: a field that can be blank will be filled in by somebody who was not there, entirely accurate about their own act and entirely silent about somebody else's, and that is not sabotage, it is a form and a hurry.** Her entry is true about her own afternoon and the day it happened is not the day it is written on. **Joon records the fill-in and refuses a meaning out loud where a room can hear him, and asks her whether she knew the field was empty**, and she says she had known for eighteen days and nobody told her not to. **The box she names is not 214, and 214 is still out and nobody can say by whom.** Vane ends the afternoon: everything needed to prove somebody altered that record is in a garage the Monday order is going to take, and the one document nobody can be given an order to produce is in chalk on a wall he does not own, and he finds it extremely inconvenient.

### Chapter 53 — Cliff Marner's Name
Sunday, day thirty-six.
