You have handed me a sentence with a door at the end of it and no room behind the door, and I have to write it down and stop, and I do not know whether I have ever written anything like that in sixteen years.** And: **the covering letter of the thirty-seventh promised the omission would be corrected as an administrative error; it has not been corrected; she has checked the file twice this month; she cannot make it be corrected; the form does not do that; in a year it will; and she is not going to be here in a year.** And the price of typing the four lines: **in four years it will read as though a lawyer found it, and a lawyer cannot be asked what she thinks it means by the people it was about, and that is the correct arrangement, and she would like somebody in that corridor to write down that she said *correct arrangement* about the thing that has cost her her month.** On the steps, **Elias says the thing he was wrong about: he has spent the week being pleased, he has been carrying a fact about their paperwork around like a shield, and it is a fact about their paperwork, and it does not make one single thing he has done since the sixth of September into a good idea — not the corridor, not the seal, not the classification, not the eleven meters, not the twenty-seven hours and thirty-nine minutes — and he had been hoping that if he could get hold of a piece of their own machinery the rest of it would get lighter, and he has known since October that is not how any of this works and has been behaving as though he did not.** Nobody improved on it for a minute, which is what it was worth. **Then Mara Okafor, flatly, once: the Wednesday is an offer, it has been an offer since the thirty-fourth day, four people are going to be asked to give something they have given every day for ten weeks, so it goes to them tonight in the words it is said in, not to him and not to Mr. Park, and she is not discussing it with him first because that is the trick — and nobody inherits her, which means there is going to be somebody in that room who says no to her out loud, and she cannot do anything about it, and she has known that since the second week and still has not worked out what she does about it.** The room was still being set out for Wednesday, nobody had sat in it, and the request was due at its near end in forty-one hours with two documents in it and neither of them in hand.

### Chapter 90 — An Answer With No Author
Tuesday, day seventy-three, the eighteenth of November, Ash Street, evening, the day before the sitting. Lena Mir is on the round at five, four carded draws in four hands, one of them Ru Sabar's with the time on it, reading the sheet as she walks. The four dark hours are five hours away. **Ansel Duarte is on his frame in the corridor at seven in the evening, awake, which means he had been awake at three.** **Cliff Marner is in the chair by the window, forty-three days out, going to a clinic in a week and not knowing it, because the driver is Ru Sabar and the swap was a night and the date had not been said in the room he was sitting in.** Della Marner is seventy-three days at a family-reception desk in Cordell with a piece of paper in a plastic wallet in two hands, one of them a stranger's, and has told nobody there the date of anything. The man behind the wall is seventy-three days in. **There was no meeting** — the Thursday decision, and Emil Baros checks the pad first with his hands behind his back and says "good" out loud to nobody, and Inez Sato says the four sentences in order because two people would otherwise do something admirable and ruin it. **The seven o'clock thing runs fifty minutes in the front room with fourteen people in it and is about one thing: an answer with no author in it cannot be given to anybody, because there is nobody to give it to.** Lena Mir: it has to go somewhere, everything gets put somewhere, the form in the tin, the page in the county library, and **you cannot have a true thing in that building that is not in a book, a wall, a file or a hand, because in six weeks somebody will come in and not remember it, and that is how things get lost that were not lost.** **Emil Baros says no column, and it is his function that makes him the man who has to say it: that column is the only thing on that wall with a heading on it and he has put his name to six weeks of keeping it, and if this goes in a column then it is a receipt, and a receipt says a thing came into this building on a date, and it did not** — it came out of a man's mouth on Friday and no paper crossed a threshold, and he has spent twenty minutes trying to find the line and there is not one, and he is not going to invent a column at half past seven on a Tuesday because the man with the pencil has run out of lines. He turns the two copies of the fourth column round and puts his finger on the column and then on nothing at all: **our copy says *items, 2*, theirs says *item, 2*, one letter, no comma, the only difference in twenty-eight lines and I found it with a pencil and a Tuesday, and the reason that letter matters is not the letter, it is that on the day somebody writes in a document this hall has a copy of its own and the Office has one of its own and the two are not the same object, and that fact holds up in a room — and it does not hold up for Friday, because Friday is not a document, it is four people in a room and a sentence and no paper, and there is nothing in this hall that is a copy of, because there was never an original**, so it goes in no book with a date, because a book with a date is a copy and a copy means somebody wrote it down and the somebody is missing, and it goes on no wall, because a wall is a place people are. Ru Sabar: **lost is not a thing somebody hides, it is a thing that was never on anybody's list.** Joon Park: every one of the twenty lines is a thing somebody is holding, with a building and a holder and a sentence about who may ask, **and that is not bureaucracy, it is a promise — somebody has it, and if you come and ask, a person has to answer you — and Friday is not that and cannot be that, because there is not a person in it.** Inez Sato says *then it is not a record*, and Joon Park agrees and writes the twenty-first line, which **has no holder, no location and no address, and is not attributed to a person, because the moment a true thing about a design has a speaker it stops being a fact about the design and becomes an opinion somebody holds**, and which ends *there is nobody to whom it can be given, because an answer with no author in it has nobody to give it to* and *not a record* — the first line in twenty-one with no location and no holder, and the only true thing on the page, and in four years somebody is going to read it and think it is a mistake. **Nobody asked who, and one person had already asked not to be asked: Tomas Vale sent a line up the stairs on Saturday morning refusing to have the finding attributed to him**, and Inez Sato says it costs him nothing and it costs him everything and both are true and she is not going to discuss it. It goes in the notebook, in the tin, band twice, on the shelf. **Lena Mir then says the finding in the room's own words — *nobody decided anything* — and that it took two days and a half and a form with a line on it and a man in a shop, and that a design that is good does not need anybody to be a villain, and that this is the same shape she has been pointing at since Tuesday, and Petar's, and the box somebody printed in about nineteen eighty when they were thinking hard about a field, and the box in the 2011 note, and the box on the 2035 form, and the line somebody added in 2003 because a night in January did not fit on the paper** — and that she is not going to be sentimental about it, and has spent ten weeks waiting for somebody to have done this to us, and there is not going to be one, and she is not going to be sad about it in front of them. **Petar Lisk writes the line that closes the batch, small, because there is not much space left: NOBODY OWNS THE ANSWER SO NOBODY CAN GIVE IT AWAY. PETAR L. — I AM NINE AND I HAVE READ IT SIX TIMES.** He reads it out twice. It has no number in it about anybody and answers none of the three questions, and the second half of the oldest one is still a bracket with nothing under it and will stay like that until somebody who is not any of us writes something under it. **Then he takes the pencil to the bottom of the board where the nineteenth of November already is, stands there for about eleven seconds, and does not write anything — nobody having asked him: there is no date for tomorrow, the nineteenth is already on there, and a hall that puts two dates on a wall in one week is a hall that is starting to expect something and then a hall that will be disappointed on Wednesday. He asks for it to be written down that he decided it on purpose, and somebody does.** **And Elias, on the stairs at twenty past ten, pays the last cost: two answers in ten weeks and he cannot spend one of them; he has stopped noticing the ward standing without him as a loss, then noticed that he had noticed, and let it go, which he has now done twice; and an answer with no author in it cannot be given away either — not to a county library because it is not a document and it is nobody's, not to a ward in another city because they would be holding something they cannot put down, not to a Deputy Director because it is not evidence about what he is doing — and it started on Saturday morning, when a woman walked into a room with twenty-three people in it and said eleven sentences and left, and four days later this building has a rule about it, and a rule is not the same as it not having happened.** A hall on Ash Street is going to be described, correctly and without cruelty, in one sentence, as the hall that kept things other people had decided were finished, and that is a true sentence; and a man came up a corridor on a board on the thirtieth day at fifty-one pounds and is in a chair by a window and has not been told about the appointment in the front room, and neither of those two men is going to be in a system this week. **Petar Lisk read the board at seven in the morning, out loud, twice, the way he does. The three questions were still on it and the date was still on it, and there was room for about two more lines, and then somebody had to build another wall, and nobody in that building was going to be the one who decided it.**

### Chapter 91 — The Third Sitting
Wednesday, day seventy-four, the nineteenth of November, ten in the morning, the inquiry room. **Rufus Aitken telephones at seven minutes to ten from the counter in another city**, reads the receiving officer's own file, and says both documents are produced and stamped, the envelope went with the regional service at ten past eight, it will be at the Meridian about ten past twelve, nobody has to come and collect anything, and **the two of them take about eleven minutes to look at and wanting more is not his department.** Rusk comes in at two minutes to ten and says before anything else that **he moved the sitting to the nineteenth because it is the near end of the team's own request, that he chose the date, that he has minuted that he chose it, and that he would have refused the date in October if anybody had asked him to hold it**, so that nobody in the room can spend three hours thinking the calendar is a courtesy. Cliff Marner forty-four days out, Della Marner seventy-four days at a desk in Cordell, the man behind the wall seventy-four days in, and nobody is going near him. **Item one, the four lines, accepted as four facts with dates and not as evidence, no finding on any of them, and Solano's name is at the bottom of it and it is the only new thing in this business in ten weeks and it is hers and not his. Item two, the chalk fourth column, entered as an exhibit and referred to nobody — and he puts it face down without looking at it, which is because he spent four minutes in front of that wall in October looking for an author and did not find one. Item three, the twenty-one-item custody schedule, entered and referred to nobody, and Mr. Park does not have to defend it and is not asked to. Item four, and it is the chapter: the Office's answer to an irregularity in a record is a process, with a step in it that somebody has to take, the step is written down, the person who takes it is on a rota, and the rota does not have his name on it and is not supposed to — and he cannot produce the form, has not been able to since the sixth of September, and is not going to pretend the process gets it. Elias finds he has nothing to say, and that this is the first time in ten weeks the correct thing to say and the thing he wanted to say have been the same length. **The envelope arrives at ten past twelve, warm from a van, and Joon Park opens it at the table because Rusk says he can, and both documents are correct and both are about a shelf.** The designation history of sector 4C from the 2039 consolidation, with the intermediate sheets: **4C was created as a storage sector designation, no intermediate sheet converts it, it has been carried forward as a storage sector at every renumbering since including 2035, and a storage sector sheet has a column for a place, a date, a volume and a unit number and no column for a person, and there is no printed space on it for a name and there never has been.** Nobody says anything for eleven seconds. Elias: *that is a category, that is not a man.* **Rusk refuses the reading that will happen in four years before it happens: a Deputy Director cannot make a holder out of a storage sector, and if anybody writes this morning down as a finding about nine men he will read it back into the room.** The holding register, the page for the eleventh of May 2039 and the page either side: **date, box, volume, from, to, unit number; three pages, eighteen rows, and no column on any of them that a person goes in.** Rusk sits down, which he had not done, and says: *record that as something I said and not as something somebody extracted. The team's question in October was who holds nine credentials. That question is not answerable and never was, because the sheet the answer would have to go on has never had a person on it. **You did not lose an answer. You asked a store a question about a person.*** Elias writes it in his notebook and stops, four seconds late: **it did not say who signed the second line of the 05:02 dispatch sheet, it said the sheet has no place to put a name, nine is still nine and nine is still nobody, and the only thing that has changed is that the team knows it asked the wrong building.** The rest of the morning: the refusal log in, the movement record in and not read, twenty-eight lines of chalk in face down, a resident's six-page statement in with its author unnamed by a procedure that has not changed since the first week. **At twenty past one Rusk says there is no finding on any of it and there is not going to be one, that it is not a trick and not mercy, that he has read them, entered them and referred them, and that he is not going to manufacture a finding to fill a morning — and that the charge is on the calendar for the twenty-seventh and Ms. Solano will have to say out loud which act, by whom and on what date.** They come out at ten to three and **Elias is glad**: about a paragraph Bernadette Locke typed at about twenty past twelve with a date on it, saying the team placed before the inquiry a form revision of this office of the fourteenth of January 2003, that the form is a designated-site incident return of this office, that the line in question is a consent line, and that the revision letter carries a form number, a revision letter, a date of issue, a district, a printed reason and no name. **That sentence now exists in a file with a case number on it, entered as a fact and not as a finding, and nobody can take it out of that file, and on Monday it existed in a satchel and a notebook and about nine heads.** It is worth more than anything he carried into the room and he cannot show one grain of it. **Joon Park: do not be pleased about the file. A Deputy Director has said out loud, on the record, that this office asked a store a question about a person, in a room with a recorder in it, and he asked for it to be recorded as something he said. If we are pleased in front of him about the other paragraph, then in four years there is a version of this morning in which a team got a Deputy Director to say a thing about his own office on the day the thing that mattered came back. — The paragraph stands and nobody is glad about it out loud. — Files do not need anybody to be glad about them. That is the whole difference between a file and a person, and it is the only advantage a file has got, and it turns out to be enough.** The sitting ends with nobody having won anything and it having been the correct outcome, and on Thursday somebody has to say out loud which act, by whom and on what date, **and Elias Rook has known since the sixth of September what that act is and has never once said it in a room.**

### Chapter 92 — The Charge
Thursday, day seventy-five, the twentieth of November. The sitting is twenty minutes with one item, headed **PARTICULAR OF THE CHARGE, STATED IN ADVANCE OF THE DISPOSITION.** Rusk says nothing will be taken as evidence and Elias is not being examined but may correct counsel from his chair. **Two particulars: that on a resident's consent card in October a consent was entered in a capacity box on behalf of a subject unable to give it, by a person who was not a physician, at a designated site, without a designation, without a physician's order, in a circuit not on any register the Office holds; and that the per-circuit consent and carded-draw system has run since the fourth of September without designation or registration, and that the first is an instance of the second.** Elias looks at the middle of the table, because the only name the first particular fits is one he wrote into a column of his own in October, and he is the only person in the room who did that. He is asked one question — correct me if I have got a date wrong — and says the date is right, and is thanked and not asked anything else. **Then Rusk says the trouble, and it is not an argument: the inquiry has to be able to say the act it is charging is not an act, and the only authority is DS-4 Revision C of the fourteenth of January 2003, requested by District 9, signed by a unit in a block, on the current set, and he cannot produce it, and he has looked twice, once alone and once with the clerk, and he has minuted that the name was not withheld from him — and there is a defect in this office's own form set, it is a defect in a set and not in a person, he found it seven days before the hearing on the twenty-seventh that decides whether this team is prosecuted, and he is telling the room now because a report in three weeks will tell them anyway. He does not withdraw the charge in this room. That is the twenty-seventh and it is his and he will take it there.** Solano says it is going in his report in the words he just used so she can object now rather than in December; she is not objecting. **Her own worry, unsaid until she says it: the second particular is the one I am worried about and I have not said so this morning.** On the landing Elias says the thing he said on the steps on Monday and had hoped was not true: **on Monday it was a thing he hoped was not the case, and on Thursday it is a thing he knows, and what he knows is that a nurse put her hand in a box in October because a man on a frame could not hold a pen and hold his shoulders, and the box is the right box, and the box is printed, and the printing is the reason the box is the right box, and a Deputy Director has just told a room the box is his office's. Being right about the Office's paperwork is not a defense of anything he has done, and it has stopped being a thing he is waiting to find out.** Joon Park on the stairs: *that is a man doing arithmetic about how much it would be worth if the paperwork were on their side, and the arithmetic does not exist, and I have watched you do it three times since Monday. — Write it down. Not for me. Write it down somewhere that is not your own notebook, because your own notebook is the only record in this business that is in your handwriting and nobody can check.* **In the front room on Ash Street at ten past seven, Mara Okafor discloses in four numbered parts, standing, to the twenty-two who pay for it, and refuses the vote.** One: in October a page of hers in her own hand went into the inquiry's file, a disclosure record of a decision about a person in a bed and a driver and a form she was not going to sign with a pen she could not hold. Two: that page is going to be read by strangers and read as the example of how this crew does things properly, because that is what makes a page an example. Three, and this is the cost: **the moment that page is an example it stops being a record of a decision and becomes evidence that we had a procedure, and if we had a procedure then we knew what a procedure was for and we did not register one, and that is in the page already. She wrote the thing that does this, her handwriting is the instrument, it is going to be a public exhibit of a process that worked and it is going to work against her, and she would like it read anyway.** Four: nobody inherits her, and she is not taking a vote, because a vote would be a way of pretending the decision is a thing the room owns. **Inez Sato, from the wall, without turning round: that is not a meeting about a form and nothing is going on the pad, and I am saying both out loud so nobody in this room can use either against her later.** Nobody writes it down. Elias understands in four seconds that the most important thing said in that room is going to exist only in the people who heard it, that he cannot do anything about that, that it is exactly the same problem as Friday's, and that it is not going to have a column and is not going to be a record and is not going to be item twenty-two.

### Chapter 93 — What Is In The Box
Friday, day seventy-six, the twenty-first of November. The far bank at twenty-five past six with an inventory sheet under one arm, because the Friday hose slot went back in a letter four lines long and the inventory half is his and has been since October. **R. Dunleavy takes the sheet at the bay door, reads it standing up, writes two figures, and says nothing at all, which is the third Friday and has stopped being an event for either of them. — Heading south. — I did not ask. — You did not ask and I said it.** Cliff forty-six days out, Della seventy-six days at her desk, the man behind the wall seventy-six days in and nobody near it. **He goes straight up at a quarter past four, which he has not done in a fortnight, because there is nothing in the building he is needed for at a quarter past four and he has begun to understand that as a fact about himself.** In the records room with Joon Park, Lena Mir, and Nadi Bell in the doorway: **he is going to do it**, and Joon says he is not going to ask what is in it. *I have four questions I ask a document — what is it, where is it, who holds it, and who put this name on it — and every document since the sixth of September has gone through those four in a corridor where the room could hear me, and two of them have had an answer that made me stop. This one has not got one. A box in a hallway under a chair is not a document. It has no location anybody can look up, it has no holder, and the man who packed it is the man it is about, and I cannot ask him who put the name on it without asking him the whole thing, and I am not going to.* **His procedure is to leave it alone, and he says that is a real procedure, the only one he has, and the reason it is his only one is that he has never had to write down what to do about a thing a person has chosen to carry. Elias: then you have a habit. — I have got a habit that has held for eleven weeks, and I would like somebody to explain to me the difference, because I have not been able to.** Lena Mir, four minutes, longer than her and nine shorter than Tarin Voss: **the interesting thing is not what is in it, it is that he has had it since the third week of September and has not read it out. A man who has got a confession in a box under a chair takes it out within a week, because a man who believes the truth is better for him cannot sit on a truth. So whatever it is, it is not the thing he would use against us. It is the thing he would use against himself, and he is not going to use it either.** Nadi Bell refuses it out loud as about four facts in a row, and Lena says it is not minuted and Joon says it is refused: **I do not have a procedure for a nurse being right.** **Elias takes it out to the corridor at half past five and works out what he thinks, and Tomas Vale is in the dry end at that moment, out of sight, doing what a man of sixty-four does in a dry end for four hours a day so he is not sitting down. He could go in there in ninety seconds. It is the only fact about the last eleven weeks that has not moved, and it has not moved because nobody pushed on it, and the reason nobody pushed on it is not the wall Inez put up on Thursday about a form and not Joon's four questions and not the rule about disclosures: it is that Mr. Vale told them on the seventeenth, standing, in eleven sentences, and was thanked and told not to thank him, and every one of those sentences was about not saying a thing in a room. If Elias walked in now and said *I am not asking what is in it and I am not here about the inquiry*, then a man who has spent thirty-one years being the one who stops other people would have to decide in about two seconds whether Elias was managing him, and he would get it right, and Elias would never in his life know which of the two things he had done.** Four walls in a month; three were a woman's decision with a date and a reason and a way of being appealed to on paper; **this one was a man's pride and there was no form on which to appeal and no version of this week in which asking him was the kind thing and also the right thing.** Nadi Bell asks what happens if he does not bring it out. **The release goes forward without it. We do not wait, we do not go and ask, and we do not build a schedule around a day when a man of sixty-four gets round to it, because that is a schedule with a man in it and we have four of those already and every one of them has cost somebody.** The documents go on the table on Wednesday: the four lines about a form, the four fields, the blank one, the two words, the 2003 revision with no name on it, the 2011 annotation with a unit number and no person. **And if he does not, then there is a release and there is no eight pages, and we do not say a word about it, and that is the end of it and it is the correct outcome and it is not a defeat.** Nadi: *you have just decided something about a man's document without the man.* **I have just decided something about Wednesday, and Mr. Vale can do whatever he likes on Wednesday, and one of the things he can do is nothing. That is the first decision I have made in a fortnight that I would be willing to have read out, and I have made it about a day and not about him, and I would like somebody to notice that and not say anything about it.** She says nothing about it, which is how he knew. **He asks Inez Sato, in one sentence, for a room on Monday morning with three people in it and nothing on the pad and no form and no vote, and says he is not going to tell her what for, and wants a room with a door that shuts.** The dry end has no door that shuts and the records room holds the schedule; take the front room at nine, chairs out, board alone, pad clean, and she will not ask and will not have been told. **Nine on Monday.** And: *you have been on the wrong side of that man's wall since the third week of September and you have just spent an hour deciding not to knock on it, and I want you to know that the second one took courage and the first one took a wall, and you are going to be asked in about four years which of those two things you were doing, and I would like it on a wall that is not this one. — Then you have somebody else's problem, and it is the only problem in this building I have ever been glad to hand on.*

### Chapter 94 — What The Office Cannot Say
Saturday, day seventy-seven, the twenty-second of November. **Elias has been up since five and has done the only thing in his week anybody has thanked him for, which is not asking a man about a box under a chair.** Cliff forty-seven days out, Della seventy-seven days at her desk, the man behind the wall seventy-seven days in, and nobody near any of it. **He has a page in his own notebook with nine days written on it, being the things he does not know, and the line at the top says *they have never withheld anything from us*, six words, his own, written on a bus coming home from a building forty minutes and an ordinary road away, and there is not one more sentence after it. That is the shape of the whole business: every true thing anybody has got hold of in eleven weeks is a line with no more sentence after it, and the reason nobody has got a sentence is that a sentence would need somebody to be able to be asked what it meant.** The records room at eleven: **every one of the eleven weeks of things the Office has said was said by two men**, and a page carrying their names is a document about Rusk and Solano, and in four years somebody reads it and says *those two were the ones who found it*, and it stops being about a design and becomes two careers, and we will have handed a man's ordinary job to the man it is about. Nadi Bell: leave the names off. **Then there is no way of knowing who said it, and a page with no way of knowing who said it is a page about a rumor.** Emil Baros puts his hand on the table and builds it over an hour and a half, **refusing a heading four times before he writes one.** Four columns is a finding. A table with a heading is a table somebody will read as a finding in about two years and nobody will be able to prove they are wrong, because they will not be able to prove they are right either, and I have been on the wrong side of exactly that twice. **Then, in capitals, underlined once: `THIS IS NOT A FINDING. IT IS A QUOTATION.`** Two columns headed *the words as spoken* and *where, when, and by whom*, and a third space down the side for nothing, so that in four years a line goes in the margin and not in the middle. **And no signature block, and his own sentence at the bottom: every line on this sheet was said in a room with a recorder in it, or typed by a clerk who types what is said. There is no line on it that anybody in this building remembers better than the person who said it.** Nadi: that is the fourth refusal, and you refused a signature and it took an hour and a half longer. **It took me eleven years at a print shop before. You get faster.** Nineteen lines by half past two. Lena reads the top of it out in the voice she uses for things she has decided are true: **put that on the board and none of you are quoting the Office, you are quoting two people, and a page with those two names on it is a page about the men and not about the machine.** Tarin, from the door: take the names off and a stranger can put words in their mouths. **Baros writes the answer in the first line: *This page quotes the Office of Continuity and two officers of it and nobody else. The officers are named. If the Office says any line on this page is not what was said, the sheet goes to a hearing and the recorder's transcript settles it, and the recorder's transcript is a thing we do not have. A claim that a document is a fake is only dangerous to the document if somebody can check it. We cannot check the transcript. So the claim is free, and we say so before the claim is made, and then we are not defending anything.*** Nobody argued with it, and he went red to the ears, which is now a thing in that building. **And then Emil Baros finds the letter, because his entire function is to know the difference between two copies by heart and nobody had told him there was a second copy. The hall's copy of the four lines says *items, 2*. The typed copy that went into the inquiry's file on the seventeenth says *item, 2*. One letter, no comma, and it is the same difference it has been since the week the clerk ran out of forms. It comes from the wall. Our copy is a copy of the clerk's chalk, theirs is a copy of the same chalk, and there is no original anywhere, and the one that is correct is ours, because I have held the wall's version in my hand about forty times. So we have got no original anywhere, and the hall may not say it is quoting the Office — it may only say which copy it is quoting.** Lena: *that is the third time that function of yours has been worth something.* **I know. I am not going to say anything about it, because I have been refused a line twice and I have stopped expecting it, and today the function was worth something and that is a Tuesday and not a pedestal.** The argument runs to five and nobody wins it. **Elias puts the case out loud at ten past five to hear whether it is true in a room: *an irregularity in a record is answered by a process, and the step is written down, and the person who takes it is on a rota, and the rota does not have my name on it and is not supposed to. That is a Deputy Director, on the nineteenth of November, in a room, on the record. And our whole argument is one sentence long: that is the correct answer to an irregularity in a record, and we would like to know what happens when the rota does not do the step, and so would the man who is on it.*** Inez: that is insulting him. **It is the only sentence I have got that is not a complaint, and it is made entirely out of his own words, and if it is insulting then he wrote the insult. And that is the whole of the case. There is no other case. We have got a quotation of our own opponent and it is the only strong thing in this building, and it is humiliating, and it is correct, and I would like somebody to write *humiliating* next to it so that in about four years whoever reads it knows we knew.** Nadi: somebody write it. **Emil Baros wrote it in the margin, in pencil, in his own hand, and then he did not underline it, and he put the pencil down on top of the sheet, which was not part of the function and which everybody noticed and none of them mentioned, and which was on the whole the most that anybody in that building had given anybody in a week.** Monday at nine, in the front room with the chairs out and the board left alone, a man of sixty-four is going to come and put eight pages on a table, and nobody in the building is going to ask him what was on them.

### Chapter 95 — The Vote Is Not Taken Yet
Sunday, day seventy-eight, the twenty-third of November, ten past ten, nineteen people in the front room and two of them on the floor. **Cliff Marner forty-eight days out, in the room, and not on the sheet, and he had asked about that on Friday and had been told and had said *right* and had not asked again. Della seventy-eight days at a desk in Cordell and is not in the room and does not know there was one. The man behind the wall seventy-eight days in.** Petar read the board at seven, twice, and said the wall was the same. **Inez Sato does not stand up and does not sit down, which is how everybody in that building knew she had already done the thinking.** Three refusals first, in order: the hall does not convene itself about a form; there is no meeting, no form, no line on the pad and no vote about the finding, and the finding is not in this room and is not going to be in this room; and then the hard one, said slowly, and she would like nobody to help her. *On Thursday this building refused to do a thing it is good at because a document was about to start a process. And this morning I am going to ask twenty-two people to decide something, and a decision is a process, and if I do this without saying out loud what the difference is then in about four years somebody will write that the hall that refused to convene itself about a form convened itself about a release in the same fortnight, and both will be true and the sentence will have no shape at all.* Nobody says anything; there is no minute of it, and the only record is nineteen people remembering it differently. **The difference: a form is a document that arrives in a building and starts something whether anybody agrees or not. A decision is not that. A decision is the only thing this building has got that is actually its own, and if the twenty-two of us put a thing out on Wednesday then nobody on earth can be asked whether we agreed except the twenty-two of us. So we are not convening ourselves about a form. We are going to take a thing that is true and ask the people who have to live in the building after it whether we are allowed to say it out loud. That is not a form. That is the opposite of a form, and if anybody here thinks it is the same thing then I would rather they said so now than on Wednesday.*** Tarin Voss: *a vote is a column with a heading and a date and a line for each person and a space at the bottom, and that is a pad, and Mr. Baros refused a column on Tuesday because a receipt says a thing came into this building on a date and it did not, and a pad is a file that has not been given a stamp yet.* **Inez: that is the best thing anybody has said this morning, and it is a reason and not an objection. The rule about the column is a rule about things that come in. This is a thing that goes out. A receipt for the thing that comes in is a lie and a receipt for the thing that goes out is the only evidence there is that twenty-two people were asked. If we do not write it down, then on Wednesday a stranger stands up and says the hall was never asked, and there is nothing on this earth to put in front of them, because we will have had it in our mouths and not in our hands. So we are writing it down, and I have known since Tuesday that we were going to, and I have been not enjoying it.** Joon Park: *it has to be read. One name at a time, and the refusal read out. That is the procedure from the sixth of September, and the four yeses went under the refusal in the same ink at the same size, and that is the only page in this whole business a stranger can read and understand, and the reason it is that page is the reading. It is the only procedure in this building that has never once been wrong and I cannot tell you why and I have been trying for two days. I think it is because it is slow enough that a person has to hear the name before their own and has time to be frightened and then do it anyway.* Nadi: that is a tempo. **It is a procedure that happens to have a tempo in it, and I am the record keeper of this building and I have been looking for a better one for eleven weeks and there is not one, and I would like the finding that a tempo is a procedure written down by somebody other than me.** Lena: it is going to cost somebody. **It is going to cost exactly two people, and I know which two, and neither of them is in this room at the moment and I have not asked either of them. And I am not going to soften it by not saying it, so: two people are going to vote no on Wednesday, and their names are going to be in the instrument, in the same ink and at the same size as the yeses, and if they voted no and we hid it then the yeses are worth nothing and the release is worth nothing and the eleven weeks are worth nothing.** Petar has been holding the pencil he has not been given, behind his back, for half an hour. *Are we going to put it on the wall.* — No. *Can I write it on the wall anyway and you can say it is not official.* **No, and I am going to tell you why, and it is not because it is not official. A vote is not a line. A line is one person writing one thing down, and that is what I have been asking you to do since September and it is good and it is the only thing in this building nobody can take. A vote is twenty-two people, and you cannot put twenty-two people on a line, and if you try then the wall has got a number on it and a number on that wall is the one thing this building has spent eleven weeks refusing to have about anybody.** — Right. Then I will not write it. There is room for about two more lines and the closing one is going to be mine and I am not going to waste it on a vote that is not a line. **Nobody asked him what the closing one was, and he did not say, and Elias noticed he had been expecting to be asked and had been left holding the answer.** Tarin: *do not do it at nine. A hall that votes at seven in the morning is a hall voting so it can go to work, and I want a hall voting because it has decided to.* **Seven. In the front room, on the chairs, board untouched, round done before it, and I am not asking a single person in this room to give me an hour of their night for this. Mr. Park takes the names, Mrs. Mir reads the no's out, I keep the instrument, and it is not in the residents' file and it is not in the tin, it is in the tin's place on the shelf with the band round it twice, because the file is the thing under a demand and the tin is ours and the instrument is going to be produced and I would like it to be a thing a person can hold.** And: *you are in the room, because you are going to have to stand in it while nineteen people do a thing you asked them for, and I am not going to arrange for you to be elsewhere.* Elias understands it in the corridor at half past ten: **he was going to ask twenty-two people to vote on a thing that was true.** Not a thing he believed; a thing that was true, arrived at by other people, in a records room, without him, that could not be improved or argued with and had no author in it, so it could be carried by anybody and it fitted in a coat. **That is the expensive part. Not Wednesday. Asking.** Nobody had asked him what it cost. In eleven weeks nobody in that building had once asked him what a thing cost, because they all knew, because they had all paid something, and the price of a thing you did not pay anything for is that somebody else pays it in the morning in front of you, one name at a time, and you have to stand in the room.

### Chapter 96 — Eight Pages *(midpoint)*
Monday, day seventy-nine, the twenty-fourth of November, ten past nine. **Tomas Vale comes up the ramp with a cardboard records box under his right arm and has carried it on the eighty bus because there is nobody in that building he is willing to ask to carry it.** Cliff forty-nine days out, Della seventy-nine days at her desk, the man behind the wall seventy-nine days in, and nobody had gone to look at him in eleven weeks. The front room has the chairs out and the board left as it was; four people in it. **He squares eight sheets against the edge of the table with his fingertips and says, in the first four sentences, that it is not what they think, that it is not a confession, that it does not explain anything, that it is a way of doing a thing he wrote in nineteen ninety-eight, that his name is on it four times, and that he is not going to read the four times out because they will find them and counting them is not a use of the thing.** He pushes the first sheet toward Mara Okafor and Joon Park and puts his hands flat on the table, and **Elias, standing by the wall because he was told to and had said *I am going to be useless in this room and I would like that minuted*, understands he has just watched a man do a version of a thing he watched him do in a records room six days ago.** It is a method: **put a held plan in front of a room in such a way that somebody who is not the author of the plan can stop it.** Joon Park reads it out, because Mr. Vale put it on the table and did not give it to anybody, and reading it aloud was the only way it stopped being one man's thing. He reads the first two and asks whether to read all of it; *all of it, in order, and read the fourth one twice, because the fourth one is the whole method and the other six are how a person gets to a place where he can do the fourth one.* **Step four, read twice: `Say out loud, before you say anything at all about the plan, that you have not told this room what you are about to do, and that you are telling them that now and not afterwards. Then stop talking.`** Mara Okafor: that is a disclosure rule. **It is a disclosure rule with the thing it is about taken out. Dr. Okafor's rule goes to the people who pay for it, in the words it was said in, before it is acted on. Mine says *before you say anything about the plan*. I wrote mine in ninety-eight because I had watched a man say a true thing in a room two hours after deciding to do the thing the true thing was about, and be thanked for the true thing, and the two hours were the whole of the damage.** Step five: `Stop talking and let them stop you. If nobody stops you, you have not done step five, and you go back to step two and take it to somebody else.` **That is the one nobody believes. Every man I have shown that page to has said *I would stop him*, and it is not about whether you would. It is that a room told in advance is a room and a room not told in advance is an audience, and the same eleven people in the same chairs do completely different things to each other. I have watched it go wrong twice. Once was a scaffold. Once was a woman, and the woman is alive, and I am not going to talk about the woman.** Mara reads the last page and then backwards and then finds his name: four times — at the top, which is a habit and not a boast; where he says the method is his and not the industry's and not this office's, having said no to two people in thirty-one years who offered to make it the industry's; in the fourth paragraph, which he reads out himself because he would rather say it than have them find it: **I wrote it down because a man ought to be able to write down how to be stopped before he needs to be, and I did not write it down**; and at the end. **And the page about a night carries no month that anybody in the room is going to say out loud, and Joon Park refuses the connection out loud, in the voice he uses for things a room can hear, and asks for it to be written down with the rest of it, and asks Mr. Vale whether there is a month anywhere on the page and says nobody in this building is going to read it out.** Elias asks, before he can stop himself, and then goes on with the rest of it because stopping halfway was worse: *you have been looking at eight pages for a thing that says where any of it started. It is not in there.* **A method, a man's name four times, a page about a night in a year, and about a hundred and forty words on step five. I have been asked since the sixth of September by four people whether I know where any of this started, and I have said no four times, and it is true four times, and I would rather you had it from me than worked out that I had it.** **And then the reversal, in his own voice: *I did not know you had that.* He had been protecting the man from a document the man had put in his own hands in nineteen ninety-eight, and the document was not about the inquiry and was never going to be found by the inquiry, and it was a set of instructions for how to be stopped by somebody who was not you — and it had been protecting all of them for eleven weeks, including Elias, and the only person who could use it was the man holding it, and he had not used it once.** Joon Park asks the question that costs: *step two says take the plan to a room with at least one person in it who was not you and who was in the room where you were going to do it. You have been in this building eleven weeks. There have been at least four rooms here where you were going to do something. Have you taken this page into one of them.* **No.** — *Why not.* **Because I do not think this building is the room where I was going to do it, and I have not once written down why, and if I had written down why it would be a page about me, and I have had enough of those.** And then, at the cost, the sentence that pays the midpoint: **I am not giving you that to use. It is not evidence. It is not about what anybody did on the sixth of September, and it is not about a form, and it is not about the twenty-third of October. I am not putting it in front of anybody's inquiry and I would ask you to leave it off anything that goes to the Meridian. I am putting it on this table because a method is only worth anything if somebody else uses it, and I am not going to ask you to use it, and if I asked you to use it that would be me using it on you, which is the entire thing the fourth step is against. So it is on the table and it is four lines of my handwriting and it is Monday, and Wednesday is Wednesday.** **Joon Park writes a twenty-second line of the custody schedule on his knee with his right hand, and Elias understands eleven seconds late that the line is new and not old.** It records eight pages, one sheet of hall paper with a printed heading, the writer's own hand, the writer present, three named — T. Vale, M. Okafor, J. Park — **no holder required, no building required, no address: a man can produce a thing in a room and then the thing is his again. Not to be recalled. Not to be produced on request by anybody, including us. Not a record. The only line in this schedule that is a person keeping a thing for himself, and the reason it is in here is that three people saw it happen.** Read back, not improved; the twentieth and twenty-first lines untouched; nothing renumbered. **Mara Okafor then says the thing that matters and calls it not a compliment: he has never been caught. In September he told the office, unprompted, that there was a 2034 write-off and a box with a number on it, and the disclosure they hold names the write-off and does not name a second thing in the same box, and there is nothing in eleven weeks of their questions that would take them to a second thing. You have been carrying this since the third week of September and nobody has come for you, and that is not because you were careful. It is because you gave them the true answer to the question they asked. A man who tells the truth about one thing and not another is not a man hiding anything. He is a man with a door in him.** — Yes. *When did you decide.* — Third week of September. About the Thursday. *That is a month of deciding.* **It is a month of deciding and eleven weeks of not doing it. Those are different lengths, and I have spent thirty-one years being the man who does not confuse the two, and nobody in this room is going to write it down, and that is correct.** He puts the eight pages back on the 2034 report, puts the lid on, and says he is going to go and stand in a corridor, which is what he does. **Nobody in the room promised anybody anything**, and that is on the page, because Joon Park wrote it there, and because on Wednesday morning at seven somebody is going to have to say out loud that they have not told the room what they are about to do, and that they are telling it now and not afterwards.

### Chapter 97 — Ninety Seconds
Tuesday, day eighty, the twenty-fifth of November, the day of Mr. Duarte's clinic. **Ansel Duarte, Lena Mir and Ru Sabar go out at about half past twelve for a clinic appointment at ten past two, two hours in a car, a wheelchair on a ramp that is not at that building, and a form Lena Mir is going to sign with a pen in her right hand because he cannot hold a pen and hold his shoulders at the same time. Ru Sabar takes it as a swap against a night and not a favor, and nobody asks Ansel anything, which was twice in six weeks. That is why Elias Rook is in the building** — he went over the bridge at half past six for the Tuesday slot, which is his, and came back at about ten, and there was nothing on his Tuesday except forty-four minutes of inventory and two figures in R. Dunleavy's own hand, and he had not gone up the ramp on Friday until half past eight. Petar read the board at seven, twice, and said the wall was the same. **Cliff fifty days out and in the chair by the window. Della eighty days at a desk in Cordell. The man behind the wall eighty days in, and nobody had gone near him since the fourth week of September.** It happens at about twenty past two in the records room, **and it did not begin.** There was no sound, no cold and no light; the room was a records room with the schedule on the table and the tin on the shelf, and then there was a doorway in the north wall, and the north wall had been a wall for eleven weeks with a picture frame on it Petar Lisk's mother brought on the second day. Tarin Voss, who was by the door because that is where he stands, said the time out loud: twenty past two, nine people in the building, eleven more asleep upstairs. **The doorway is a door's width and about seven feet high and it is in a wall that has never had an opening in it, and beyond it there is a street.** Elias has been asked four times and gives the same four sentences: **a street with buildings on both sides and no people on it and rain falling on it, and the rain made a sound like rain; a smell of rain on a road with grit in the surface, and under that a smell of hot brakes, and there was no engine making that smell, and the far end of the street did not get smaller.** He did not step toward it; he has been asked about that too. **What happened next took about eleven seconds and happened without him.** Somebody said a number out loud — nine in the building, said by Tarin, and **nine in the records room and the front room and the corridor, said by Nadi Bell from about four feet inside the doorway** — and then a hand went to the card rail in the front room and came off it with a card on it, and the name was read out and written on the round sheet with the word *out* against it, and the card went into a pocket and not back on the rail. *Mr. Lisk is in the dry end. Card's in my pocket. Not on the rail, so in about four minutes somebody has to decide whether to look for him or not, and the October answer is not to, and I am not going to change the October answer in a doorway.* Joon Park, from the terminal, unsteady: **there is a rule and I did not make it up and I have wanted to make up one every day for eleven weeks. If a card is on the rail its holder is in the building and accounted for. If the card is not on the rail the name goes in the round sheet as out and that is the end of it, and the round sheet is not a record of where anybody is and never has been. Nineteen of us agreed that in October and nobody has to be asked about it now.** Nobody wrote it down then. It is on nineteen pieces of paper. **The terminal and the corridor repeater print one line at about twenty-three past two and Elias reads it aloud, because that is the practice:**
```text
Warning: the branch is changing
```
**And there is nothing under the line. I want to say that out loud, because in eleven weeks every one of these has had something under the heading, and this one has not, and the nothing under it is the first new thing anybody in this building has seen since the sixth of September. It is not an offer. It is not a loan. There is nobody in it to ask and nothing in it to refuse.** **He does not say *I hear you*.** He had said that once, in September, into a relay, to a man nineteen years older than his sister, and had got a rule back instead of a gift and had been right to. **And nobody goes through the door.** Tarin Voss was in the frame and did not go through it and did not go near it, and said afterwards that he had wanted to and that wanting to is not an argument: **the reason nobody went through that door is not that we are brave. It is that nineteen people agreed something in October without being asked and I was one of them, and being in a frame for ninety seconds is not courage, it is a procedure, and I would like that said before anybody in this building gets proud about Tuesday.** At about twenty-one and a half past two the doorway was not there, the frame was on the wall and the wall was the wall. **Ninety seconds. Nobody went through it, nothing was asked of anybody, and the whole of the price was that nine people spent ninety seconds of an ordinary Tuesday not doing something they would have thought about every day for the rest of their lives.** Petar comes out of the dry end four minutes later with a book in each hand, does not ask, looks at the rail, is shown the card, says *right*, and asks whether anybody had been frightened; **nineteen people said yes in four different ways over the following hour and a half, and he wrote nothing on the wall about it, and the wall had room for about two more lines and the closing one was going to be his.** **Nadi Bell, an hour later: for eleven weeks I have been waiting to find out whether that building stands up because you are in it. It does not. It stood up tonight because nineteen people agreed something in October without being asked, and none of them are you, and one of them is a nine-year-old who was in the dry end with a book in each hand, and he was not even in the room.** Elias says nothing for about a minute. **The cost he had been paying since the fourth week of September arrived, and it arrived in a records room at twenty past two on a Tuesday afternoon, and it was that he was not needed, and it was not a loss.** In ten weeks he had been given a great many things to carry and every one had a weight, and the thing with no weight was the one that was true. **He had spent his whole career learning to be the person in the doorway. On Tuesday afternoon the doorway opened and the doorway was a room where nine people were already doing it, and he stood in it, and there was nothing for him to add, and nobody looked at him, and nobody was wrong.** He went up the ramp at four and did not tell anybody that it was the best hour of his week, and that was the first time in eleven weeks he had not told somebody something. **The door is on no drawing. Joon Park says so at about five with the round sheet in one hand and the schedule in the other, in the tone he uses for things he has checked twice: no plan of that building in four places, the wall is solid on every one, and the picture frame is on a nail in a stud. Nobody photographs a doorway they are not allowed to photograph and nobody goes out on the street to see whether the street is there. What I am going to do is write it down, in as many hands as will write it, and put one copy in Cordell and one at the depot and one in the county library's local-history room and one in my own notebook, and none of the four is the record and the record does not exist and the four of them are four people being frightened in four different buildings, and that is the whole of what I can do and I am going to do it anyway.** **In the end it was nineteen hands, in chalk, on the wall, in the space under the clerk's fourth column, and it took until about eleven that night, and by Sunday the nineteen hands were in four buildings.**

### Chapter 98 — The Release
Wednesday, day eighty-one, the twenty-sixth of November. The round is done before seven, the chairs are in the front room, the board is left exactly as Petar Lisk read it at seven, out loud, twice. **Twenty-one people in the room are on the sheet, a twenty-second name is in the room without its body, and a twenty-third person is in the chair by the window and not on the sheet at all. Cliff fifty-one days out. Della eighty-one days at a desk in Cordell, not in the room and does not know there was one. The man behind the wall eighty-one days in.** **Inez Sato opens with step four, out loud, in the room, before she says one word about the release: I am going to do a thing on Monday that Mr. Vale wrote down in nineteen ninety-eight, and I am going to do it before I say one word about the release, and nobody is going to have to be told that this room is a room and not an audience. I have not told this room what I am about to do, and I am telling you that now and not afterwards. Mr. Vale wrote it on page four of eight pages he put on this table on Monday, and he told me on Monday not to ask you to use it and I have not asked and I am not going to, and if you want to know whether it worked you can decide that yourself and not tell me.** Then: the vote is on the release, not on the finding, nothing on the pad, Mr. Park has the sheet, Mrs. Mir reads the no's out, twenty-two names one at a time in the order they are on the rail, and if a person is not in the room their card is not counted. It takes about an hour. **Petar Lisk read the board at seven and then sat on the floor at the back with a book, which is where he has sat since October. Ru Sabar's card had gone down at ten past six the night before, as it does, and she was in the room, and she had told nobody she was going to be, and nobody asked. Mr. Lisk's card is on the rail, so he is in the building. That is the October rule, and it is the only rule in this building that has never once been wrong, and I am not making an exception for a boy of nine at seven in the morning. He is not voting on a document nobody is going to ask him about. Joon, read his name when you get to it.** **The vote is twenty in favor and two against and nobody abstains, and Joon Park writes down after the fourth name that the answer is in the sheet and not in the count. Four of the twenty-two give a reason out loud and the other eighteen say nothing, and the instrument records the four and the count and not the eighteen, and Lena Mir reads all four out loud at the end in the same voice, without comment, the way she reads a round.** **Ansel Duarte**, back from the clinic on the Tuesday at about five: *I said in November that the arguing is the appointment and that the hazard is my legs. I am voting yes and I want it written down that my reason is that on Tuesday afternoon a thing came through a wall in this building and I was forty minutes away at a clinic, and this house did not need me, and I have been waiting eleven weeks to be able to say that out loud.* **Osei Kwarteng**: *My wife is asleep in the next room on a battery the clinic charged forty-one dollars for, and nobody in this room knows where that money came from, and it is not her money and it is not mine and it came out of a tin. I am voting yes and I want it written down that I know what it cost and that I am not going to be thanked for it and that if somebody thanks me I am going to hand it back.* **Nadi Bell**: *No. And I have been quiet about this for ten weeks and the reason is that nobody asked me, and I would have said exactly this on the ninth day. Dr. Okafor's rule goes to the people who pay for it, in the words it was said in, before it is acted on. She is right about the rule and I have watched her apply it four times and it is the best thing anybody has done in this building since the sixth of September. And I am voting no because a release is not a decision about a person, it is a document, and the rule was never written for a document, and Elias Rook said that out loud in a room in November and was right, and what we are about to do is ask twenty-two people to hand a refusal of a document to a room that is going to write it down, and the first of those is me, and I am not going to pretend to a room that I have been comfortable with that for ten weeks. Nobody is to soften it and nobody is to ask me twice.* **Ru Sabar**: *No. I am not going to explain it and I am not going to be asked. A condition is not a safe place. A condition is a thing you can break, and I break mine every night at ten past six, and that is the only power I have got, and it is not even a good power. And the day there is a copy of a condition is the day somebody can come and ask me whether I agreed to it, and I will not be able to prove that I never agreed to anything because agreeing is not on any of my cards, and I would like that written down.* **Mara Okafor is standing by the wall and does nothing at all for about eleven seconds: thank you, Miss Bell, that is a rule of mine being right and not being enough, and I am not going to say a word about it in this room, because a word from me in this room would be a word from the person the rule came from, and then it would be the rule arguing and not a person voting. To Ru Sabar: I am not going to thank you either and you are not to hand anything back to me, because there is nothing to hand back, you have not given me anything. Nobody inherits me. That includes the people who vote no.** **Then it goes forward, with two no's in the same ink and at the same size, the instrument on the shelf in the tin's place with the band round it twice, not in the residents' file, and anybody who wants a copy can have it in this room and cannot have it out of this room.** Four people put a page on the table. **Elias puts down the four institutional fields** — a field that does not exist, a field that can be blank and gets filled in by somebody who was not there, a field nobody designed for the thing in question, and a field that exists and has nowhere to attach — **and the blank field, the 14:12 line from the day of the collapse in a depot's aisle log, blank on the day it was made and filled in five weeks afterwards by the archive floor's supervisor out of her own recollection, eighteen days after the column was restored. Joon Park puts down the two words, *not applicable*, filled in on the fourteenth of April 2035 in a printed box that asked about a field and not about a person, by a clerk nobody has put in anything.** Tomas Vale puts down the revision letter and says out loud what it is: a form number, a revision, a date of issue, a printed reason, a district and a block with no name in it, a line that says a person can be entered on behalf of a subject unable to give it, and a form in a drawer that cannot be produced by anybody including the Deputy Director who convened the inquiry. **Then eight sheets with a printed heading: those are mine, I am not offering them as evidence, and I have said that once already this week. They are on the table because a method is worth nothing if only one person has it. Nobody in the room read them; that had been arranged on Monday and Elias had made himself stay by the wall, and it held.** **The one interruption is Emil Baros, and it is about a letter: I have to say a thing, and I am not a person in this room and have never claimed to be, and I have got a pencil, and if I do not say it now it goes in wrong in a document about this building. Our figures and theirs are not the same document. Our copy of the clerk's fourth column says *items, 2*. The Office's says *item, 2*. One letter, no comma, twenty-eight lines, and I found it with a pencil and a Tuesday six weeks ago. And I have traced where that letter comes from and it comes from the wall. Our copy is a copy of a stranger's chalk, and theirs is a copy of the same chalk, and there is no original anywhere on earth, and the version that is correct is ours.** He puts the pencil down on the table, which is not part of the function and which nobody mentioned. **So the page has to say which copy it is using, and it must not say it is quoting the Office. It is quoting a chalk wall that a man in an orange jacket ran out of forms to write on, and a file, and this hall — and I have told you the file and this hall are not the same object as the wall.** The instrument's fourth page carries that sentence; it is read out, it is agreed, nobody votes on it because it is not a decision, and **Lena Mir writes it out longhand because a typed page would be a page in a file.** One more thing, in a plain envelope, handled by nobody. **Nadi Bell puts it on the table herself: it is a movement record, six pages, written in a past tense on her own authority in October about people she has never met, about a thing that has not happened, and the tense was hers and she chose it and said at the time she would be judged on it. Enter it as testimony. Do not enter it as a finding, and I am not going to explain why. A finding is a thing somebody decided and a movement record is a thing somebody wrote. In four years there is going to be a file with a finding in it saying the record was not evidence, and I would like the record in that file first and called a record.** Joon Park writes it and reads it back: **the record is entered as testimony, it is not a finding, it is a movement record in a past tense, it is on the shelf with the band round it twice, and nobody in this building is going to be asked to read it out.** It was twenty past nine when the room emptied, and **Elias stands by the wall where he has been for two hours and a quarter and does not pick anything up.** The chapter closes on the payoff of a promise made six chapters ago on an Office landing: **Joon Park told him to write something down somewhere that was not his own notebook, because his own notebook is the only record in this business that is in his handwriting and nobody can check it. There was not one line of the instrument in his handwriting. There were four names and two reasons and two refusals and a sentence about a chalk wall, and nineteen hands on the wall underneath the clerk's column, and four refusals read out loud in Lena Mir's voice, and his own hand had been on a chair and then on a rail and then in his pocket. He had asked twenty-two people to vote on a thing that was true, and had stood in the room while they did it, and had been the least important person in it, and that had been arranged on Sunday by a woman who had told him in a corridor that asking was the expensive part, and she had been right, and it had cost about nine seconds per person, and he was the man who had asked.**

### Chapter 99 — What It Costs
Thursday, day eighty-two, the twenty-seventh of November. **The folder is thinner than the folder on the Wednesday, and that is the first thing Elias Rook notices and the thing he spends the morning trying not to be glad about. Cliff fifty-two days out and has not been told there was a sitting. Della eighty-two days at a desk in Cordell. The man behind the wall eighty-two days in, and the seal on the tunnel is nine words and a date, and nothing in the room that morning touched either.** Rusk: *Counsel, put the charge as you have put it three times and then I will dispose of it.* **Solano puts it: the crew of the Kestrel response, on the face of a notice served on the thirty-fourth day, entered a consent on behalf of a person unable to give it, on a resident's card, at a designated site, without a designation, without a physician's order, in a circuit not on any register the Office holds. A particular act, a particular month, a particular signature in a particular capacity box.** Rusk: *Mrs. Mir, you are in the room because your name is in the second particular and not because anybody asked you to be.* **I am aware of what my name is in. One thing, and then I am sitting down, and I would like it in the words I say it in. I put my hand in that box in October because a man on a frame could not hold a pen and hold his shoulders at the same time, and I would have put it there again that afternoon, and if you take the charge off I would like it recorded that the only reason I am sitting here relieved is that you took it off for a reason about a form and not because anybody in this room thinks I should not have done it.** — Recorded. **The charge falls.** *It falls because this office cannot bring it without explaining its own form set, and the form set has carried a printed row since the fourteenth of January two thousand and three for entering a consent on behalf of a subject unable to give it, and the inquiry cannot produce the form, and an institution that prosecutes an act it cannot show is a thing a court throws out and a citizen should be able to throw out. That is the whole of it, and it is a bad reason, and it is the true one and there is not a better one hiding behind it.* Solano: *I have an objection. The charge should fall because the act was not a coercion and the practice was disclosed and the circuit was run on consent cards that any household can withdraw from with no notice. Your reason is that the paper is old. Mine is that the paper is the only thing in this building that anybody has got wrong about, and I would like the finding to say so, and if it does not I am going to write a dissent of about four hundred words and it is going to be read by somebody in about four years.* — Then write it. — I intend to. **Elias: in October you told me a Deputy Director's office is not built to hold that question. It is not built to hold a second one either, and you have just said so in a room with a recorder in it, and that is twice.** — *I am aware of the count.* And he does not improve it and does not put a number on it. **Findings, read in full and fair, and the reason fair is that this office will have to defend this file in about four years to somebody who was not in the room. One: a records management finding — an irregularity in a record is answered by a process, the step is written down, the person who takes it is on a rota; the form revision of the fourteenth of January two thousand and three is referred to that process; and the finding is that the form cannot be produced, and that is a fact with a date and not a finding about anybody. Two: three endorsements are revoked, effective today — Dr. Okafor's medical advisership, Mr. Rook's and Mr. Vale's designated-site advisor's endorsements — and that is the cheap thing and I am presenting it as exactly that. I have known since the fourth of October that I could do it and I have held it for seven weeks, and the reason I did it is that it costs me nothing, because an endorsement is not a license and a license I could not have touched. It is neither cruel nor generous. I am doing it because it is available, and I am saying so in a room with a recorder in it, so that in four years nobody can describe this office as punishing anybody. And there are men in this building who thought about it every morning, and they know why.** **Mara, first: I want the exhibit read into the record. The disclosure page that is in my hand, which is going into a file as the example of how this crew does things properly, and I want it read out, and I want it called what it is: a process that worked. I said in October that I would say that and I am saying it, and I am not a traitor to a team for saying it, and Mr. Rook can put that in his notebook with a date on it and I would prefer it in somebody else's notebook than his.** **Elias's endorsement, and then a second thing that is not Rusk's and that he reads because the department asked him to:**
> **HAVENPORT RESCUE SERVICE. Re: E. Rook, field technician. Suspension from seam assignment of the forty-first day is EXTENDED, case reference HC-42-0417 remaining open. The department's position is that the individual is not a field technician this month. Hose certification and inventory, depot, far bank, Tuesdays and Fridays, 06:30. These slots will not be held twice.**
*I asked for that to be read aloud, and I am going to say why, and the reason is that in about four years this file will be read by somebody who was not in the room and it will look as though a Deputy Director ended a man's career in four paragraphs. A Deputy Director wrote one paragraph. A man in a depot wrote the rest, and the man in the depot is right, and the department has a form for this and the form says the individual is not a field technician this month, which is a sentence about a month and not about a career, and it is the most accurate sentence anybody has written about you in eleven weeks.* He lets it sit. **That is the best thing that has happened to me in nine weeks, and it is a loss, and I am not going to explain it in a room.** — Nobody asked you to. **No. And that is why I said it out loud, and I would like the recorder to have it, because I have spent eleven weeks in rooms where nobody asks me what a thing cost, and today a man read a form out loud that says the correct thing about me for the first time since the sixth of September, and I cannot pay it back and I cannot use it and I am not going to be the man who says thank you for it.** **Tomas Vale: the endorsement is revoked, and the second part is not in Rusk's gift and is said as a fact and not as a favor — the file will record that Mr. T. Vale is not available as a structural authority on a designated site. Not suspended. Not under review. Not available, in the past tense, in a document, forever, for any site that carries a designation. It is one line and it goes on the form in a box that already exists, and it is the only identity you have left and I have just taken it, and I am telling you that I know what it is.** Tomas, looking at the table for about four seconds: **Then it is in a box with a form in it, which is where I have been putting things since the fourth of September, and I have no objection and I have one condition: that I am not consulted about any designated site by anybody, in writing or out of it, and that if anybody does consult me they are doing it because they have to, and I will not be able to help them, and that is the correct outcome and I would like it in a box with a form in it too.** — It will be in the file. **Then Rusk does the thing nobody was expecting. Mr. Vane, the designation line for a person recovered from a designated site. I am going to make one now, in a room with a recorder running, and I am going to say out loud first that a form does not do this and will in a year, and a Deputy Director can, in a room, and that if it is done wrongly it is done wrongly in a document that exists, which is the whole argument of the last eleven weeks in one sentence. Not a passenger. A person recovered from a designated site under an inquiry reference. Case reference HC-42-0417, nineteenth of November 2042. The name goes on it, spelled as it is on a chalk wall in four hands on Ash Street and as it is on a case extract produced by a transit operator in another city, and I am saying where the spelling comes from, because a designation with a misspelled name in it is a thing that gets used against the man it names.** He writes it out himself, which nobody had expected, and signs it, and gives it to Bernadette Locke. **Elias's objection: it should have been done on the nineteenth of November and it was not, because this office's covering letter of the thirty-seventh day promised the omission in the equipment list would be corrected as an administrative error, and it has not been corrected, and I would like the word *error* to stay out of this entirely.** — It stays out. Counsel, note that the designation is a field created on the twenty-seventh and that the covering letter of the thirty-seventh is unfulfilled and will be fulfilled in about a year, when the form can do it. — I will note it, and I will note that the form cannot do it, and I have been saying that since the seventeenth of November. **Then: Mr. Park, run a search. On the patient named at line six of the Kestrel return, in this inquiry's own system.
