I have got two and I have never been able to do one single thing about them, and I would have told you in June if you had asked me in June, and I am telling you now because you did not ask me in June.** And the thing he does not do: **there were four people dead in this city for twenty-eight days and no piece of paper in the world said so; there were four doors on that block where nobody had been told anything, and there was a woman in this flat who had gone to four of them on the night, politely, with a torch she did not need, and she had written their names in a book, and the book had gone somewhere, and the only place on earth where the four names were still written was half an inch of margin in a different pencil.** He could have gone to four doors; he had the tube, the notebook and a bus at twenty past five. **He says it out loud once, in his own voice, to a man who did not need to be told anything: I am not going to use these for four days. I am not going to go to their doors today, and I am not going to use one line of this in a room tomorrow night, and I want it said out loud to somebody before it is said out loud to a room.** — *Write it down,* Joon Park said, and did not write it down. **They get the 11 at ten past four, and over the low road where the water comes up in a hard winter he sits with a cardboard tube across his knees and four names in pencil on the back of a hand that was not his, and does not open the tube, and it is the hardest physical thing he has done in a district that has been handing him easy things all week.**

### Chapter 110 — The Number He Says Out Loud

Monday, day ninety-three, the eighth of December, seven in the evening. Cliff sixty-three, Della ninety-three, the man behind the wall ninety-three. **A program leaflet on every chair in the church hall off Marden Row, about two hundred people, and a microphone on the folding table that worked.** **She had put the leaflets on the chairs herself: Marged Prosser, fifty-five, chairs a parents' group in this district, lost a son in the 2039 outfall, and is the reason a regional pilot program's leaflet went into a school in September and not only into letter boxes — because she went to the school and asked, and a woman in a high-visibility vest told Elias Rook on a Saturday that it was the cleverest piece of administration anybody in this city had done in ten years.** She starts it at four minutes past seven in about ninety seconds and then sits down beside the table, which is not a thing a chair of a parents' group has to do. **Tobias Ferrand is fifty-two, was a duty flood officer in 2027, 2031 and 2036, is Regional Director of the Bellwether Pilot Program, stands at the back for four minutes listening, and is brought up by name because *we are going to lose the hour*. He is better at it than anybody in that room including the man who had come to speak.** He does not take the microphone off the table; he holds it at chest height and says the first four things into his own collar. *I am not going to stand here and tell you the program is a good thing, because there is a leaflet on your chair and you can read it and it is a good leaflet and I did not write it. I am going to take questions, and I am going to answer all of them tonight, including the ones I do not like, and if I do not know one I am going to say I do not know one in this room rather than in a letter. So. Ask.* **And he asks the round himself, which is what makes him good at it: he goes down the room asking what people want to know, writes nothing down, and answers nine before anybody else speaks.** A man of sixty and his garden; a woman and her postal address; two teenagers and the school — the board's decision, not his, asked and told in writing that the school is not in the alignment, and he gets half a sentence into saying so and stops. **A man in a wheelchair by the aisle: I lost my wife in the outfall in '39. — I know who you are, Mr. Pritchard. I signed the letter. — I do not want a list I have to argue with. I have got one. It is in a drawer and it is four pages and every name on it is right and I still cannot get anybody to say a sentence about it. If you put four hundred names on a piece of paper and they are all right, what have I got. — You have got a piece of paper, and I am sorry that is the answer, and I am going to come back to it.** And then, unprompted, after a pause of about a second and a half: **Two hundred and eighty-five addresses in this district are classified `TRANSITIONAL` on the schedule I signed, and I am going to say that number twice tonight, because I have noticed that people would rather hear a number twice than hear it once. Two hundred and eighty-five.** Those addresses are not in the retained district; that is a fact; it does not mean anybody has been told to go anywhere, and it does not mean there is a destination, and I am not going to stand here and be clever about the difference, because the difference is real and it is also the whole difficulty. **Then four things, all true, and he wants the people at the back to hear all four. One: the reading is right, it has not lost anybody in the field, and I will put that in writing to anybody who asks me for it in writing this week. Two: the schedule is the only document anybody in this room can defend, and a schedule you cannot defend is a guess, and I am not going to be the man who asks the people in this room to act on a guess. Three: I signed it, on the second of December, in the Meridian, with my name on it, and it is my signature and not the program's, and I will sign it again in the spring when the next reading comes, and I would like somebody in this room to understand that I am not a messenger and I have never been a messenger, and if I am wrong about four hundred people then four hundred people find out on the morning they start, in front of everybody. Four: the second reading came in on the twenty-ninth of November and the band moved two streets; forty-four addresses in this district are inside the September reading and outside the November one; I signed the schedule on the September reading; I have not rebuilt it, and I have not been asked to, and I am not going to pretend to you that I do not know what that sentence is. Here is why, and you will not like it, and I would rather you had it from me in a church hall than find it in a fight: if I rebuild that schedule every time the band moves, then every time the band moves I am rehoming four hundred people in an afternoon. There is no version of an afternoon that is not bad. People die in the moving. There is a foreman in this district whose entire job is to make sure this district does not have that happen twice, and he is better at it than I am, and I am not going to overrule a foreman at eight o'clock at night in front of the people who live here. So I signed it on the reading I had, and I signed it again knowing what I know, and that is mine, and if you want to be angry with somebody about it, I am available on a Tuesday and I am not going to be offended.** **Marged Prosser then asks the best question anybody asks all night: what is the difference between your reading and your schedule, and which one is the wall going to be built on.** — **The wall is built on the alignment, and the alignment is the board's and not mine, and the board chose it on a flood model from 2039 that they have adjusted twice and told anybody who asks. My document is not the alignment and I am not going to stand here and pretend the two are the same shape, because they are not, and because I do not think the board would thank me for saying they were.** — And which one says whether I am still living here on the morning they start? — **Yours does, and mine does, and they are not the same document, and the honest answer to your question is that I do not have a third one.** **She writes that down on a card, and she is right to, and she is going to be right about most of what happened next.** And then the worst thing Elias does all week, from the floor, with no microphone, knowing what it is while it is happening: **Tobias Ferrand. Has anybody in this program ever been wrong?** The room is not silent; it is worse, because about forty people turn round. **Can I take that into the microphone. — Not to make a point: a question asked in a room deserves to be heard in a room.** He repeats it exactly as asked. **Yes. Twice, that I can produce, and I have both in writing and I will give you both. The first was in 2039 on the eastern bank, where a block of addresses came off the survey with an error in it and I signed it and people were told the wrong thing for six weeks, and I was right about the ground and wrong about the rows. The second was this one. I am not going to be modest about that. I got this one wrong the day I signed it, and I knew I was getting it wrong, and I signed it anyway, and I have just told you why, and I would like you to write down that I said it in that order and not the other order.** Somebody at the back says *oh* in a way that is not a heckle. **Then a woman on the front row leans over, puts the microphone on the table in front of Elias Rook, says nothing, and sits back down. Four minutes. Nobody speaks. A man in a wheelchair looks at the floor. Somebody at the back moves a chair. The hall, with a door open onto a cold evening, is extremely loud with nobody talking in it, and Elias stands in an aisle between the chairs with a folder he has not opened and does not fill the four minutes, and is extremely conscious of the fact that he is not filling them, which is the worst part, because the not-filling is visible from twenty feet away. Ferrand watches him do it, waits him out, does not rescue him, picks the microphone up off the table.** And the sentence that closes the evening, without any edge at all, which is the cruelty of it: **Mr. Rook. You have been in this district nine days. I have been in it nine years. You have a page in a notebook and I have four hundred and twelve addresses that are still standing. Not one of them is on any list of mine that I have had to explain to a coroner. And I will say this. If you would like to make the schedule a better document, I will get you the working folder — the one with the drafts in it and the September overlay and the thing I sent back twice — and you can have it on Monday, and I am not doing it as a favor and I would like it noted that it is not a favor, because I would like this program to be checked by people who are not in it.** — *Thank you,* because there were two hundred people in the room and it was the only one available. — *Monday.* And he puts the microphone down. **He goes into the yard at ten past eight and does not say one word to anybody about what he thinks and does not write it down either, and stands by a skip for twenty minutes while a district empties out around him. The inside back cover of the notebook has two dates on it from Friday. The back of the week — Thursday, Friday, Saturday, Sunday, Monday, five days, five pages — has one line on it, and it is a date, and it is the near end. 2 JANUARY. The whole of what he had learned in nine days about a program that had a name and a face and an accurate reading of the ground fits on a line on the back of a page in his own hand, and the line is a date that somebody else was keeping for him.** **And that night the district learned that there was a man from the Cinder Quarter who had come nine times and asked good questions, and a man from the cell who had come once and answered all of them, and the first one went home on the 11 and the second one stayed and shook about forty hands at the door.**

### Chapter 111 — The Issue Slips

Tuesday, day ninety-four, the ninth of December. Cliff sixty-four, Della ninety-four, the man behind the wall ninety-four. **Depot Tuesday at half past six is his; the Friday inventory half is on the notice and empty in his week.** At number six Anum gives him **two yellow slips, one per occasion, ref BWP/TG/4SD/2042, TAKEN 11 SEP 42 06:40 with clearance 05:50–07:05 ISSUED 11 SEP 42 08:10, and TAKEN 29 NOV 42 06:15 with clearance 05:50–07:30 ISSUED 1 DEC 42 09:40, both signed N. PEART, YARD.** The reader is vehicle-mounted and the street has to be shut by the works. **He notices the eleventh of September was a Thursday, five days after Saturday the sixth, does the subtraction in his head and does not write it down, because a man with four names on somebody else's hand has no way of knowing whether he has got one right.** Peart confirms he moves his own metal at ten to six and signs for the road. **The line moved off Sallow Court and onto Marlow Street, so the forty-four are not all the same kind of wrong; some retained addresses are inside the new band and on no list.** The two streets four inches apart have been known to him since Thursday the fourth of December, when he counted the forty-four off the overlay with a finger. He rides past the street of a man of sixty-four without getting off.

### Chapter 112 — A Room, As She Asked

Wednesday, day ninety-five, the tenth of December. Cliff sixty-five, Della ninety-five, the man behind the wall ninety-five. **One day left on the Sunday-to-Thursday rule.** He books the room off the canteen in his own name and says step four first. **Hobbs shuts the door herself: she builds to a bearing off a 2039 survey adjusted twice, has never seen the pilot's sheet, knows where the wall is and does not know where the ground is, and will not take that further than that.** She confirms his Saturday stop was right because nine strangers would have believed a folder. **Her clerk signed the 3 to 27 Sallow Court clearance against the reading; she did not sign and has not signed anything since 2036.** On the eighth of November the works cleared a block to something dated the second of December. **She asks for a sentence said out loud in a room with the door shut about whether the line moved after it was given, written down in words nobody will be embarrassed by in three years.** He tells her he has something in a kitchen margin with one day left and she says she did not ask. **The only man who could say the needed sentence is sixty-four, not available, and is not asked.** He books it himself and walks her out to the gate, and the batch closes on the cost in the batch's own unit: **twelve days and about sixty-six pounds of fares at two pounds seventy-five each way.**

### Chapter 113 — Nineteen Years Ahead

Thursday, day ninety-six, the eleventh of December. Cliff sixty-six, Della ninety-six, the man behind the wall ninety-six. **The four-day rule runs out tonight; the four stay in a margin and on the back of Joon Park's hand.** He tells Hobbs the rule and the limit in a room with the door shut, then stands on the public side of the alignment at Sallow Court with his right hand on brick. **The volume's first interface notice arrives, in the approved shape, and is accepted in the same chapter: Loan offered Tomas Vale nineteen years ahead, Load Reader, direction and stress and not a repair, boundary the reading is mine to give and the list of who is on it is not, return tell me out loud in a room whether the line moved after I gave it to you, with Give it the season and I am not asking for the district under it.** The present man is sixty-four in another room in this city and is not asked. **He says yes out loud to an empty street, reads force off Sallow toward Marlow for four minutes, learns nothing he can write down, and tells Hobbs the offer in the words it happened in before acting on it.** The line moved. It is his word and not a document.

### Chapter 114 — What Cannot Leave a Room

Friday, day ninety-seven, the twelfth of December. Cliff sixty-seven, Della ninety-seven, the man behind the wall ninety-seven. **Friday depot half is inventory, on the notice, empty in his week; he does not go.** At the counter his standing observer request of the first of December still has nobody free. He tells Anum step four and puts his hand on the trestle: **the thread is live here, quiet over the water, off in the Cinder Quarter; a proximity and not a voice, it does not tell him where anybody is.** **The reading is the same one as Thursday and not a thinner one, and being sure of it cost him nothing, which he understands at the time to be the shape of the trap, and nothing he learns in that room can be written down and taken to a counter. The five limits are not restated here; they were stated in Chapter 113. The two middle knuckles lock for an hour and a half.** On the bus he thinks about the terrible use — **a man with no form and no date whom a district believes could become the schedule, putting unchecked readings into kitchens by Monday** — and does not do it and tells nobody he stopped. At Ash Street Joon notes **fourteen days running** and a Friday with nothing in his hands. **Petar reads the three questions at seven in the evening; the wall is full.**

### Chapter 115 — Three To Twenty-Seven

Saturday, day ninety-eight, the thirteenth of December. Cliff sixty-eight, Della ninety-eight, the man behind the wall ninety-eight. **Board and counter shut until Monday; yard runs six days.** Alagoa reads Rennard, Murrow, Marlow at the bollard for an hour, Sallow Court last, about twenty listening, none thinking it remarkable. **The yard gate carries the closure timetable behind glass, open to closed, public side standable until the new year, and this is the first time he has stopped to read it; a season is a window.** At the gate pier he tells Peart what he is about to do before he does it, puts his right hand on stone, finds the thread live: **the ground on this block went quiet twenty days after four people died on it, the ninth of November to the twenty-ninth, a fact about force and not about anybody, nothing he can write or hand over. Peart gets the arithmetic right, says he would have said a month and would have been wrong, and will not put ground in a book for what his crew did.** **Aftermath for about an hour shows stress he is not touching, a load that is not that building's load today, cannot be refreshed or willed; nosebleed and a lost hour; hand locked again.** He washes the glove, bins the plaster, counts fares and sleep and travel in that order: **fifteen days at five-fifty a day round trip, eighty-two pounds fifty, four more before Thursday.** **Hall holds twenty-eight on twenty-two cards.**

### Chapter 116 — What I Have Not Told This Room

Sunday, day ninety-nine, the fourteenth of December. Cliff sixty-nine, Della ninety-nine, the man behind the wall ninety-nine. No bus, no counter open. **Petar reads the board at seven in the morning, three questions untouched, second half of the oldest still a bracket, nineteenth of November still the only date, no room left; Cliff in the chair by the window; twenty-two cards and twenty-eight people; four dark hours ended at four.** He tells nine people step four with nothing behind it: **the Thursday offer, and that he has already given the terms to Hobbs on Thursday and Anum on Friday and that the terms are why he is telling the room and not the counter, the line off Sallow onto Marlow, what it will not do and will not will, the hour of wrong-load sight afterwards. The boundary and the return are not said a second time.** Petar asks to be shown and is told no. **Joon says to the room that wants a sentence about a seam: we do not know that; what happened to Elias is not a record; if asked he will say a man put his hand on a wall.** Inez confirms people who pay first. Mara telephones: **the Marden Row chemist still rounds out of his own van, told 25 November transitional cannot be billed, out of money mid-January, about sixty affected; clinical and administrative, the moral point not hers.** Envelope unsent, reason four words.

### Chapter 117 — Sent Back Twice

Monday, day one hundred, the fifteenth of December. Cliff seventy, Della one hundred, the man behind the wall one hundred. **Day one hundred falls here and not on any earlier page; checked twice.** Counter open nine to half past four. **Ferrand's working folder arrives with a scene: drafts with crossings-out in two inks, September overlay dated by a clerk, and the thing sent back twice — one page from the regional office dated 1 December listing the forty-four with proposed TRANSITIONAL to RETAINED and a check box, stamped RETURNED CANNOT BE ACTIONED ON A LATER OCCASION 1 December and 2 December, annotated twice in Anum's hand A schedule is drawn once. Re-issue on the next occasion, and **a printed tally line at the foot of the page reading `IF ACTIONED — TRANSITIONAL 241 · RETAINED 456`, set against the schedule's own 285 and 412 out of 697, so that the forty-four is derivable by anyone reading both and is said out loud by nobody.** Next occasion the fourth, January, date not fixed inside the contractor's window. He counts forty-four again without putting schedule and rolls together. **It is not the verification log and shortens nothing.** Joon logs the sighting in the margin, no twenty-third item. **Petar: still no room.**

### Chapter 118 — The Shutters On Cutbank Row

Tuesday, day one hundred and one, the sixteenth of December. Cliff seventy-one, Della one hundred and one, the man behind the wall one hundred and one. **Depot Tuesday half past six, Dunleavy's book, twenty minutes; hour of sleep a week for life.** Shutters half up; Postridge and Co. queue and nail. **Counter: standing request 1 December still none free; Wendy Tallow, thirty-four, regional pilot observer under the August clause, hand on the counter log which is not the verification log nor the clearance record nor the shift book.** Upstairs a typed query from the board's surveyor dated 16 December asks the date of the reading the schedule was built on; **a copy of the nineteen-page schedule goes up with it, first time the document has left the district.** He tells Anum what he is about to do before he does it, and reads the damaged stair rail: **it is not a repair, the stair shakes under his hand the whole two minutes, and it has told him nothing whatever about the woman at the counter.** In the corridor Wendy asks a fair question — **has Hobbs asked you for anything in a room** — and he owes an ordinary professional reply. **He says no. It is a lie in a corridor to keep a shut-door room out of a counter book.** He tells Joon in the words said. Joon: you lied to a program officer. Yes, and not explained.

### Chapter 119 — A Reading You Cannot Repeat

Wednesday, day one hundred and two, the seventeenth of December. Cliff seventy-two, Della one hundred and two, the man behind the wall one hundred and two. **Query and copy reached the Meridian sixteenth and the desk seventeenth; pass-through, not a decision.** On Marlow Street with his hand on brick he finds the thread live but changed: **the edge softened by half a street with no reading taken, no vehicle, no shutdown, no drawing — a change nobody caused.** A changed future can alter the thread before use, and nobody stated that cost to him in so many words and no form in the city states it: **from tonight the after part is the easy part to see and the hard part to put back.** **This is the small instance and it is not Chapter 134, which is larger and is brought on by his asking the boundary to be repeated, where 119 has no action behind it at all.** He practises the return sentence to nobody. **Church hall half past three, about thirty parents, kettle, working microphone; a man asks for the ground in one sentence and Joon says we do not know that — schedule, reading and a hand on a wall are three things and the third is not a record.** Marged writes on a card and keeps bringing the young man who says when it is not a sentence. **Yard gate closure timetable read a second time, and the board figures 1,140 and 2,900; Sallow notice still there.** Ash Street at seven, wall full, fares and sleep and money in that order.

### Chapter 120 — A Document With A Date On It

Thursday, day one hundred and three, the eighteenth of December. Cliff seventy-three, Della one hundred and three, the man behind the wall one hundred and three. **Counter nine to half past four, locked at twenty-five to five; room above number six booked to six; log stays downstairs.** Anum leaves the rolls and slips and goes down. **Rusk, fifty-nine, Deputy Director, first appearance in this volume, comes at twenty past five, shuts the door, says step four in his own mouth.** He tells them in a room: **the pilot authorization is a document with a date on it, August, authorizing a method and not a conversion nobody asked for, and nobody in the Meridian will defend one; he has read the nineteen-page schedule of 2 December with the italics footnote, does not like it, and cannot say so in his own office — the first thing anybody in the Office has said about the schedule.** No ruling, no answer to the query, only what he read and where he cannot say it. **Elias does not open the folder and does not trade the admission back in a corridor; does not put the four into the parents' card index, on a woman's consent and not a moral argument.** 11 home, thread quiet over water. **Verification log requested 5 December, near 2 January far 16 January, nothing back, nobody asks.** Wall full at seven. Thread live only on asked-about ground.

### Chapter 121 — Two Names On One Card

Friday, day one hundred and four, the nineteenth of December. Cliff seventy-four, Della one hundred and four, the man behind the wall one hundred and four. **The depot's Friday half is on the notice and empty in his week — a tick for every week of the year and no name — and it is a rota fact and not a cancellation. Twenty days, twenty fares, £110.** The works' sequence on the wall planner, eight nights, the January four in a different pen: 21 and 22 and 23 and 24 December, then 6, 10, 17 and 19 January. Peart: nine, eleven, eleven and the rest; the woman at 31 Cutbank Row wants to be last on the seventeenth. **He takes the card wallet off the nail and the wind takes the sleeve door and the worked pile off the second nail, and a card off the night of the sixteenth for 9 Cutbank Row has a woman's name and a man's name added under it at the door in the dark in a different ink, and he reads the address and the first name and stops, and puts the card back upside down, and Peart squares the pile the right way round without a word and knows which card it was.** The stair at number six shakes the whole time he has his hand on the rail and tells him nothing. An old woman with a barrow tells him what a crew that will not be rushed on a stair is, and that it cannot be put in a form. **He works out in ninety seconds that a reading is not hands, that there is one person he could ask, that a question is a hand put out, and that he does not have the rule and it is not his to invent. He does not telephone her.**

### Chapter 122 — The Rule Is Four Minutes And It Is Not A Courtesy

Saturday, day one hundred and five, the twentieth of December. Cliff seventy-five, Della one hundred and five, the man behind the wall one hundred and five. **Offices shut, yard open, run day twenty-one.** Alagoa at the bollard with about a dozen, and he walks past her because he has something else to do, and takes away that a woman of sixty-three said a number out loud in a street and nobody thought it remarkable. On the telephone in the corridor at nine he tells Mara he is going to ask on Tuesday, and gives the name. **The practice is worked out twice wrong and right the third time: tell the other one you are going to ask, not the ask; then stop; then count four minutes; silence is a no; a no is a no; a yes after four minutes is a no with a bad memory. Mara has already caught him doing it to rooms instead of to people, and caught him once for rehearsing it and once for making the four minutes a thing she had to fill.** Petar writes it in the book on the page after 22 November and it does not go on the board, because there is no room. Inez: a rule with two people in it is worth exactly two people, and the day it goes into a room with a form on the table it becomes a procedure. He gets as far as the second line of what he would say to the woman who has not met him and stops.

### Chapter 123 — How You Tell A Woman Of Eighty-Four

Sunday, day one hundred and six, the twenty-first of December. Cliff seventy-six, Della one hundred and six, the man behind the wall one hundred and six. **No bus, yard shut, counter shut. The run stands at twenty-one days and £115.50 with two Sundays out of it.** Petar reads the board at seven in the morning; three questions, bracket empty, 19 November the only date, wall full, no second wall. The four minutes are in a book and not on the wall. Inez on what a rule becomes when it travels. Joon rewrites the four names in ballpoint because the pencil went out on the fourteenth: **forty-two days.** He will not put them on paper and neither will Elias, and they stay in a margin and on a hand, on no schedule, with no number, to be named in January out of her margin. Joon's we-do-not-know-that, applied to asking a person rather than to a seam. **Rhoda Mistry, forty-seven, clinic manager, telephones the hall at half past two and asks how you tell a woman of eighty-four that she is being carried out of her house on Wednesday night.** Ninety-one people in eleven weeks with a letter, and it is a count of people and not a count of doors. **She has eleven names on the back of a discharge list, three years old, and has been asked for them once, by a man from another office who wanted to be complete, and said no.** Elias says he will come to the clinic in the morning and will not ask on a telephone.

### Chapter 124 — A Form In A Drawer Since August

Monday, day one hundred and seven, the twenty-second of December. Cliff seventy-seven, Della one hundred and seven, the man behind the wall one hundred and seven. **Run day twenty-two, twenty-two fares, £121; travel then sleep then money, in that order.** The clinic at ten past two: Mistry's conditions, and the list goes to the works and to nobody else — not the cell, not the program, not a regional office — and she has said no once and was correct. **Peart copies the eleven onto eleven works cards in his own hand, writes a floor and a stair on each, signs all eleven, and calls for a ladder out, not a carry, one at a time, with a man at the bottom of it.** Hobbs in the room off the canteen with the door shut before he has it open, the eleven cards put down one at a time and no name read aloud, and she tells him what is going to happen: **within a week there will be a piece of paper in that building with a heading and a line for the answer, and he will be tempted, because a woman on this street has kept a list in her own hand for eleven years and been asked for it once, and it turns out that being asked is most of what there is.** He tells her the terms of what he is going to do before he does it and makes the promise about no list in that room. **Then Selwyn Anum's form, out of a drawer since August: `SECURED SUBJECT`, `SUBJECT HELD`, a signature and a date and nothing else, no field for whether the subject agreed, so the answer goes on the back.**

### Chapter 125 — Two Hundred And Eighty-Five Addresses

Tuesday, day one hundred and eight, the twenty-third of December. Cliff seventy-eight, Della one hundred and eight, the man behind the wall one hundred and eight. **The volume midpoint. Depot at ten to six, Dunleavy's book, twenty minutes, hour of sleep a week for life. Run day twenty-three.** Room above number six booked half past nine until one. Joon at twenty past, Anum at half past and standing, Wendy Tallow at ten to ten **because Elias asked for her, because the people who pay are in the room, and he knows that while he is doing it.** Mara at ten past ten. **He tells her he is going to ask and stops and counts, and at two minutes and forty seconds she says yes — to being told, inside his four, and tells him to do it in the words he would use on a stranger and not to put the page in it.** He puts his hand down hard on the trestle and asks the question out loud in front of five people, and it takes eleven seconds, **and the question is about the eleven and not about him and not about her.** Joon does not move; Tallow's hand goes to a notebook and stops there; Anum says his name in a voice with nothing in it. **The offer arrives, and the panel is verbatim: Mara Okafor twenty-one years ahead, Still Hand, keep one living body from failing while another person carries or repairs it, do not use my hand on anybody who is on a list and do not use it to make a schedule come out right, return when the last one is out of the building and you say out loud that it is the last one.** He reads the terms out to the room — step four, named by Anum, in the wrong room — and reads the boundary twice because the schedule is open at page two under it. **He asks the one question he is entitled to ask, and the answer is the refusal, in her words, in the room, before anything was done: you have written down what happens to them before you have asked me, and I have spent twenty-one years refusing to write a rule and only ever writing a decision, and there are two hundred and eighty-five addresses on that page and I have not met one of them.** Tallow's log entry, four lines, in ink, present tense. **Anum writes it on the back of his own form because there is no field for no, and says the first honest sentence anybody in that pilot has produced is there because he did not build a box for it.** **Mara refuses him in front of the room: you asked her about them; you kept the rule and then asked a different question; I am not entitled to be angry at a woman twenty-one years ahead of me who has answered for her own capacity and I am not going to be grateful to her either, because being grateful is the same shape of theft as being angry. Nobody inherits me.** Joon at the door: you have lost the cover of a rule you helped make. The thread survives and nobody asked it to and nobody took it.

### Chapter 126 — Nobody Was Hurt That Night

Wednesday, day one hundred and nine, the twenty-fourth of December. Cliff seventy-nine, Della one hundred and nine, the man behind the wall one hundred and nine. **The last working day of the year. Twenty-four days, twenty-four fares, £132, two Sundays out of it and this is the last one.** Ash Street, full at nine. **Elin, regional oversight, out on the 11:40 on Christmas Eve, with a one-sided form, eleven ruled lines and a box at the end of each; three answers already in the program's own voice, and the third box wants a word in it and there are six words that would go in it and every one is wrong.** She fills it herself in ink and reads it back: **Declined, with a reason, in the room, before any use** — five words, and she says why, and the reason is that a form that lets a program take a decision out of a woman's mouth and leave the reason behind is not a form. **She asks him whether he is going to do it again and he gives her the true answer, which is that he asked a woman in a room about eleven people instead of about himself, in a room with her officer in it, and read the terms out afterwards like a man performing a step.** The room's address goes in the margin in ink and **Joon refuses to copy it and will not have it near the file in the next room, and a woman on that row has kept a list of doors in her own hand since 2031 out of nothing but being asked once, and that is her street and not his.** Yard: `YARD CLOSED 25, 26, 27, 28 DEC · REOPENS 29 DEC` typed along the bottom of the closure card. **Eleven cards went out on the wallet at nine on Wednesday morning and eleven doors went and nobody was hurt.** Hobbs with four photographs of 9 November, twenty to seven, from the north corner, and not one line in any schedule she has ever written for the man with the torch. **The booking: twenty-eight working days, Monday 5 January to Thursday 5 February, four nights left in it, two of them Saturdays, and after the fifth of February the board has to come back for money and in her experience does not come back in the same year it has already spent it.**

### Chapter 127 — The Whole Thing Was Still There

Thursday, day one hundred and ten, the twenty-fifth of December. Cliff eighty, Della one hundred and ten, the man behind the wall one hundred and ten. **Christmas Day. Yard, counter, board, hearing room and determination office all shut, and it is on printed notices and not announced. The run stops at twenty-four days and nobody in the building marks it.** Petar reads the board at seven in the morning, out loud twice, and says it is Christmas Day and the whole thing is still there, and that he has a tin of biscuits in the front room and the tin is not a rule. Marigold Truscott's turkey in a blue blanket she means to have back in January; eight of the twenty-eight worked on it in two kitchens; Petar carves it badly; Ru Sabar will not say where she ate the same thing in 2039; Cliff is fed a third of a plate by people who do not ask. **Elias could eat most of it because a woman of sixty-three read a thousand doors out loud at a bollard a week ago and a man of fifty-one copied eleven names onto eleven cards and signed all eleven on Monday, and neither of those had anything to do with him.** **Mara is not in that building and the reason is Saturday night. He does not ring at ten and does not ring at two and rings at ten past four; two minutes forty.** Nobody was hurt, and it is not his, and he is not allowed to be proud of it in the voice he is using; he asked a woman in a room on Tuesday for hands he did not need, and the two facts are going to be in the same sentence whether he puts them there or not. **She tells him not to go looking for somebody to be angry at so that it stops being his, and that there is nothing to be forgiven and that is the part that will be hard in January.** A boy of nine wants a story about somebody with no powers at all, and asked whether the man was brave. Joon says the two dates in a room on Christmas morning because nobody asked him to, and complains that the second of January and the sixteenth are both Fridays.

### Chapter 128 — The Two Dates And A Sheet With Four Rows On It

Friday, day one hundred and eleven, the twenty-sixth of December. Cliff eighty-one, Della one hundred and eleven, the man behind the wall one hundred and eleven. **Boxing Day. `CLOSED 25, 26, 27, 28 DEC · REOPENS MON 29 DEC 09:00` in a plastic holder on the inside of the cell's door. The depot's Friday column: a tick, no name, the same as every Friday, and a fact about a rota and not about a holiday.** The letter is one side on regional letterhead, typed, signed **Tobias Ferrand, Regional Director, and it contains no figure at all**: the authorization carries a date in August and stands and the method is not withdrawn; **the Retention Schedule of 2 December is to be reduced to an operational list**; `SECURED SUBJECT` and `SUBJECT HELD` are program headings and a gift name is a gift name and neither is a finding about anybody; the verification log was requested at a regional counter on 5 December with a near end of 2 January and a far end of 16 January and no expedite, and the log is the only document the office holds that can answer the question the board's surveyor asked on the sixteenth. Joon makes him say the two dates in a room and says the request stands and nothing has come back and nobody has chased it. **Joon also says out loud that Elias is not going to reduce it either, and that he expects him to go quiet and thoughtful for a fortnight and come back with a page in his notebook that is very good.** Petar brings the file of working logs down off the top shelf on Boxing Day because a letter came, and not because a man of thirty-five came home and looked interested, and will not open it at a table with nine people in the room. **Elias lays two fingers on the box and nothing at all comes up through it, because the ground under Ash Street is not the ground anybody is being asked about, and he does not look.** Inez: the fourth January night is a Monday, the nineteenth, and the two before it are Saturdays, and nobody knows why. **The arrangement: no Tidegate address out of this building, the sequence stays in this building, and he will read it out in the front room on Sunday and take it no further than that.**

### Chapter 129 — What She Reads Out Loud On A Saturday

Saturday, day one hundred and twelve, the twenty-seventh of December. Cliff eighty-two, Della one hundred and twelve, the man behind the wall one hundred and twelve. **Counter shut again; the run is over and he is not going to invent a reason to start it.** Alagoa at the bollard with fourteen people, Rennard and Murrow in a different voice because he walked them in December, **Sallow Court last and thirty-nine doors, and about eight of the fourteen stop listening and two do not.** A man of sixty whose mother is on Rennard and the even side and has been since 1971 and is not on the sheet, and she tells him so in the street in December and will tell him the same in January. A woman of forty at number eighteen since 1988 who has never wanted a number and now would like it to be the right one. **He stands still this time, and he puts the restraint before the number and not after it: two is a fact about two pieces of paper and not about anybody, and he is not going to be the next voice on that corner. She says the gap of two is not hers to correct, because a tape would make her a woman with a grievance on her own road.** **He has the four dates in his coat and does not give them to her: she has not asked, the reason the four stay in her margin is her consent and not a moral argument, and a document handed to a third place without the woman who owns it is a thing you do because you would like to be the one who did it, and it would have cost her the drawer.** Joon has copied the four names out again in ballpoint, forty-eight days, and lists the four things this building could have done in four weeks and has not done, and says he is the only one in it keeping that list.

### Chapter 130 — What You Would Have Had To Do

Sunday, day one hundred and thirteen, the twenty-eighth of December. Cliff eighty-three, Della one hundred and thirteen, the man behind the wall one hundred and thirteen. **Yard shut, counter shut, board shut, and the card behind the glass says twenty-five, twenty-six, twenty-seven and twenty-eight and means it.** The file of working logs goes back on the top shelf with the 1998 one at the bottom, unopened, and Petar says it is the correct place for it on a Sunday. The four minutes are in a book; **the refused question is on the back of a form in a building in another district in a method officer's ink, because there is nothing on the front of it**; the two dates are in pencil on a forearm; the four dead are in a margin and on a hand and on no schedule. **He reads the works' sequence out in the front room with thirteen people in it: four rows, four nights, four crews, no addresses, and he says out loud what the document is and is not, standing up, so that this building cannot later say it did not know, and there is not a Tidegate address in that room and there is not going to be one.** Marner calls it the third most useful thing anybody has done in that building since September and refuses to ask for the other two. **Then he tells them the answer is four men and a torch and about forty minutes a woman, and that a lamp is not a man who has done it before, and that he is going to be on that ground on those four nights with a lamp in his coat.** Joon, without a pen, deliberately: **what would you have had to do** — *about eleven seconds* — and makes him say the sentence he will not be able to un-say, and he says it: he would have put his hands on eleven people he had never seen, off a list, on a night, in a building, in a room with a form in it, having already invited a program officer to watch, and called it keeping them alive. **The only thing standing between him and it was a woman in another year who had said no to a page.** Inez closes the lock-box. **The lamp: second-hand out of a box of eight, a pound and something for the lamp and a pound ten to run, bought Christmas Eve, fitted on the 26th, tested on a dark stairwell on Boxing Day, not on the residents' file, not on a pad, not in a book, not near the power budget, and deliberately not on a table in the front room, because a lamp on a table gets voted on within a week and the day it is voted on it is the building's.** The notebook's two dates and one line under them: *Asked on the 23rd for hands I did not need. Told on the 19th. Everybody who did it: a doctor, a foreman, a clinic manager, a man with a ladder.* The yard opens on Monday the twenty-ninth, the booking starts on the fifth of January, and nothing whatever has been done, and a lamp.

### Chapter 131 — Five Door Numbers

Monday, day one hundred and fourteen, the twenty-ninth of December 2042. Cliff eighty-four, Della one hundred and fourteen, the man behind the wall one hundred and fourteen. **The yard reopens and the closure card is still in its frame with the typed line on it, and nobody took it down, because there is no procedure that makes a person take down a notice once the notice has stopped being about the day.** **The fourth reading is fixed for Thursday the first of January** — street shut from twenty past six, reader on the alignment at a quarter past seven, slip back about eleven — and Anum fixed the date on Friday in one line without consulting the works, and says the reading will not care who is standing on the street, and nobody asked Elias to be there. **He asks Nerys Hobbs, in the room off the canteen with the door shut, to be on the 6 January block, and she refuses**, because there are four men who have done it forty times and he has done it once, and because the night a sheet goes wrong is the night somebody reads her name and not his; she gives him a fifth of what he asked for, which is the bottom of a ladder, the doorway, and being four minutes slow out of the way. **He walks 14 to 22 Marlow Street in daylight: five door numbers, and a woman at number eighteen with a folded pram tells him there are nine kitchens behind them, and tells him why she is telling him, and he does not open the notebook.** Peart tells him what a lamp is for: a door. **Twenty-one doors, four men, and nobody has ever asked a foreman how he manages that, and the reason nobody has is that they think a door is a household.** The run is over and is not restarted; a fare, not a day.

### Chapter 132 — Three Minutes, And Then She Was Not Going To Be In It

Tuesday, day one hundred and fifteen, the thirtieth of December. Cliff eighty-five, Della one hundred and fifteen, the man behind the wall one hundred and fifteen. **Depot at ten to six, Dunleavy's book, two figures, twenty minutes, the sheet pinned flat with the right forearm, an hour of sleep a week for the rest of his life.** **He tells Mara Okafor at eleven that he is going to ask, stops, counts, and gets a yes at three minutes — and then she says she is not going to be the person he tells what it means, and that is not a rule and she is not writing him one.** She is at the health center until four. **The Marden Row round: eleven of sixty invoiceable through the center because the center has already seen them, forty-nine not, and a committee in the second week of January that has not read a letter.** **She stops him in the corridor when he starts to make the forty-nine into a thing he is right about**, because a letter in words a man from the Cinder Quarter has chosen is a document somebody can wave. **Peart: two of the eleven are on the 6 January block, and he will not say which doors and will not say which stairs, and the answer is that a man with eleven doors and no list is the most dangerous thing in that district.** Joon telephones from a payphone and refuses to say the two dates on a telephone.

### Chapter 133 — A Notice With No Addresses On It

Wednesday, day one hundred and sixteen, the thirty-first of December. Cliff eighty-six, Della one hundred and sixteen, the man behind the wall one hundred and sixteen. **The last working day of the year.** **Nev Peart has pinned up a printed works' night schedule on the gate: four January nights, four blocks, four crew numbers, no address anywhere on it, and a line reading `Printed by the yard. Not a program document.`** Six copies to Mrs Postridge at 2 Cutbank Row after three weeks and two arguments and a lawyer. **There are no door numbers on it because a printed sheet a newsagent hands round, with the block's nine door numbers on it, lets a person at the gate write down which house a crew is going into on a Tuesday night — and the twenty-one in this yard is twenty-one lamps in the store, not twenty-one doors.** The woman at thirty-one will read the seventeenth on Saturday like everybody on that row and he cannot take the nail out. **Hobbs rules the first line of her four-line sheet for 6 January, writes the date and the crew on it, and does not sign it, and says in her own mouth that a sheet about a street is signed in the morning by whoever was standing on it when it was walked — she tells him the works' notice is the only document about this ground written by the people doing the work and pinned up for anybody in the street to argue with, and warns him that the day door numbers go on it is a program document.** And in the rain at the gate he works out that the four January dates are on a nail and a woman will have them on Saturday without him, and the only part she cannot have is which house, and the only copy of that is on a kitchen table on Cutbank Row, and **he is not going to ask for it, and the reason is no longer a rule he made in December and was told off for making late. It is that he asked a woman in a room on the twenty-third for a thing, and eleven people are going to be hurt in the second week of January because of it, and he is not going to put a second version of that sentence on a table.**

### Chapter 134 — Two Times On A Page And Nothing Between Them

Thursday, day one hundred and seventeen, the first of January 2043. Cliff eighty-seven, Della one hundred and seventeen, the man behind the wall one hundred and seventeen. **New Year's Day and the yard is open, the counter is open at nine, the board is open, and nobody in that district shut anything for the day.** **THE VOLUME'S THIRD AND LAST INTERFACE NOTICE, AND IT IS A BOUNDARY ONLY**: he is on the north corner of Marlow Street with his palm on the brick at **06:10**, ten minutes before the barriers go up, and the panel that comes back has the boundary in it and three empty fields. The street shuts at 06:20; the reader is on the alignment at 07:15 and off it at 07:50; the output is on the trestle at a quarter to eleven with the check box signed. **The securing band has come off Rennard and off Cutbank Row and gone two streets back the way it came in September, and forty-four addresses change class, every one of them `RETAINED` on 2 December and every one of them on Rennard or Cutbank Row.** Hobbs tells Anum to write it in his own hand, because in February the only sentence anybody will believe comes out of the hand of the man whose name is on the slip. **Anum fills the check box and dates a one-page reissue on the first of January which does not withdraw anything, and the December sheet's printed line `IF ACTIONED — TRANSITIONAL 241 · RETAINED 456` is now wrong and nobody crosses it out, because there is no hand in the room with the standing to do that to another office's printed figure.** **Hollis Vane, about sixty, nineteen years the custodian of exhibits, has come from the Meridian on purpose because a copy of the schedule went into his file on 17 December inside the board's envelope and an exhibit can only be superseded by a document that says which document it supersedes and when.** And he says the thing once, in his own words, about his own trade: every document he keeps is a picture of a day, and there is no field anywhere in his Office for *what a picture was a picture of on a day that was not the day it was taken*, and nobody designed a form to be asked that, and the fault is in the cabinet and he is the cabinet. **There was a second thing in the room that he did not say. At ten past four he asks only two questions: what time the reader was on the alignment, and what time he was on the ground, and Anum answers both and refuses to read a foreman's book out in a room because it is his.** On the bus he writes two times under the two January dates and nothing between them.

### Chapter 135 — The Second Of January Is A Friday And A List About Crews

Friday, day one hundred and eighteen, the second of January 2043. Cliff eighty-eight, Della one hundred and eighteen, the man behind the wall one hundred and eighteen. **The near end of the records request, and nothing has come back.** Wendy Tallow: a near end is a service target and the date on the estimate when it was taken, the estimate said four to six weeks, a near end in four and a far end in six is a service telling you the truth about itself, and what has not come back is an explanation she is not entitled to give; the far end is the sixteenth and she says it because he has read the dates in a room twice. **Nobody chases it and nobody asks Joon.** **Tobias Ferrand comes in person for twenty minutes with no folder and issues the operational list** — one page, `NIGHT / BLOCK / CREW / DOORS`, ref `BWP/TG/4SD/2042/OP/1`, no address, no class, no person — and says four things: the authorization carries a date in August and stands; **the Retention Schedule of the second of December is held, is not withdrawn, is not superseded, and no further schedule is to be issued against that reference**; this is what a crew works from; **two hundred and eighty-five, one thousand one hundred and forty addresses, about two thousand nine hundred on a model adjusted twice, nine years, and he is not rebuilding the schedule and on Tuesday night he is going to empty a block on Marlow Street with four men.** He does not deny the 1 January page and says he has nothing to say to the forty-four that would be true, and says that in February there will be no line in any document of his with Elias's name on it and that he can live with that. **Then the terrible use, in a cold corridor, put side by side on purpose: a page with forty-four addresses and a thread that tells him when a man is near. He does not do it, and nobody tells him he was right to stop.** Hobbs will not sign the list, will sign her own sheet, and will tell the board in February that the crew went to nine households and the list said five.

### Chapter 136 — What A Notice Does Not Carry

Saturday, day one hundred and nineteen, the third of January 2043. Cliff eighty-nine, Della one hundred and nineteen, the man behind the wall one hundred and nineteen. **The counter is shut behind a locked door, the board is shut, the yard is open, and all three of those things are on paper and were announced to nobody.** Peart: Mrs Postridge has sold four copies and one went to a man on Rennard who has been telling the street since Friday that the seventeenth is Rennard, and Peart cannot correct a man on a street about a notice he put up himself. **Hobbs's sheet has four ruled lines and not one signed; two men in an office have asked her to add a line for anything left behind and she has said no twice, because a line for items left behind turns a sheet about a street into a sheet about a suspicion.** **At the bollard, Winifred Alagoa reads the notice four times and says the thing nobody in the program said in nine months: it is a true thing and it is on a nail and it does not have December on it — and the woman at number nine Rennard who went out on the twenty-second with a bag and her cat in a basket is on no nail and in no list, and Alagoa is not saying one word to her and is saying it here because here is the only place a sentence of hers has ever gone anywhere.** The woman of forty at 18 Sallow Court asks whether thirty-nine is the right one and is told no, it is the one she has, and that she would not say so even if she knew. **A woman of seventy tells Elias he is not a man who knows and keeps it, and tells him not to stand there like one, and he discovers that he has been coming to that corner to give things away and has mistaken stopping for listening.** He goes and does nothing on purpose for forty minutes.

### Chapter 137 — A Lamp On A Table Gets Voted On

Sunday, day one hundred and twenty, the fourth of January 2043. Cliff ninety, Della one hundred and twenty, the man behind the wall one hundred and twenty. **The yard's one shut day in the batch, and the only one of the four closed places that has a piece of paper on it, and its paper is out of date.** Petar reads the board at seven, out loud twice; bracket empty, the nineteenth of November still the only date, no room, no second wall. **A works lamp has been on the table in the front room since Saturday afternoon because the light over the stairs has been out since Friday, and Inez Sato says a thing on that table is a vote and she will not pretend otherwise. She says her two refusals, current, and neither of them touches a lamp, and asks for it minuted that she said the word document and then said no anyway.** The hall votes at half past ten: **nine for, four against, one abstention, eleven pounds and fifty of a float of about twenty.** It goes in Petar's book and not on the board. **Petar asks why the lamp in his coat is not on the table and is told, and says *you are frightened of the vote* and is told *I am frightened of winning it*, and ten minutes later his version of the arrangement comes back through the front room slightly wrong with an extra sentence in it, and Elias does not correct it.** **Joon Park refuses to ask what happened on Thursday morning before that street shut, and gives the price of asking — that he would be the fourth person in the world holding it and the fourth person is the one who eventually says it out loud in a corridor — and says he has charged nothing for that and it is not generosity.** Petar asks him what the sixteenth of January is, in a room, and is told, once. **Petar's second list has carried a name since 2029 in his mother's hand; the 1998 box stays on the top shelf; Elias does not go up and says so in about nine words.** The four dark hours come and he has four men, twenty-one lamps, a sheet with four unsigned lines, a lamp, and a notebook.

### Chapter 138 — Two Documents On One Wall And Neither One Taken Down

Monday, day one hundred and twenty-one, the fifth of January 2043. Cliff ninety-one, Della one hundred and twenty-one, the man behind the wall one hundred and twenty-one. **The first working day of a twenty-eight-working-day booking, twenty-eight working days from 5 January to 5 February with the yard shut Sundays, and after the fifth of February the board has to come back for money.** **Peart pins the program's operational list up beside his own sequence, eight inches apart, and takes neither one down**, because his sequence is the only record of what the yard will actually do, and the day the two stop agreeing is a Tuesday in February and it is not his to decide which is right. **The `DOORS` column and a card for every door have never once agreed: a range of numbers is a street and a card is a door, and a flat has a door and does not have a number on the street.** **The board's surveyor, unnamed, third visit, is given the three reading dates in ninety seconds by Hobbs — 11 September twenty to seven, 29 November quarter past six, 1 January quarter past seven — writes them in a hardback notebook, says *that is three occasions and my query is one date*, walks the alignment end to end in an hour and ten minutes, and on his way back writes one word and turns the notebook round: Present.** **Peart gives Elias a job: count the door numbers, not the people, and write the five on the back of your hand, and tell nobody.** **Hobbs will not fill in the form for a person present and names the cost — a foreman's word and a fifth man's word against a form — and the old reason is that a man who is not on the sheet is a man who can be sent away, and she would like four households on that block to be able to send a stranger away without a form in their front room afterwards.** At four o'clock she says two things: Rennard on the tenth and Cutbank Row on the seventeenth, and **thirty of the forty-four reclassified addresses are on Cutbank Row and the rest on Rennard, and the crews are going to empty both those streets in the next three weeks, and she is going to do it anyway and is not going to do it well, and both are true.**

### Chapter 139 — Five On The Back Of My Hand

Tuesday, day one hundred and twenty-two, the sixth of January 2043. Cliff ninety-two, Della one hundred and twenty-two, the man behind the wall one hundred and twenty-two. **The first of the four January nights: 14 to 22 Marlow Street, crew four, half past five in the afternoon to twenty to seven in the morning, which is thirteen hours standing on that road, of which the night's own window is eleven hours and ten minutes, from the first door at half past seven to the barriers down at twenty to seven.** Barriers at six, the lorry with the ladder and four lamps, the crew's briefing, and a foreman who walks the block at a quarter past six and says nothing to anybody. **Doy tells him where to stand and why: in the way, because he will not go where he is told and Doy would have to argue with him in front of five doors.** He is moved out of the way, holds a barrier arm, carries a flask, and walks to the shop and back for bread and butter. **He does not go near the inside of a door. The interiors of those five doors are four men's business and his business is a road.** At 20:30 a crew lamp battery goes flat, the spare is a hundred yards away on the lorry, and **he gives them the lamp out of his coat and holds the frame for about two hours with his right hand while two people in overalls get one person out of a first-floor flat and into a vehicle, and Peart sees it once from the middle of the road and looks away.** At about four in the morning two men spend an hour at one of the five doors and he does not know which and does not ask, because two of the eleven are on that street and the only record of either of them in the world is a card in a wallet in a drawer and a ballpoint list at a clinic. **The street is walked at about half past five. Nobody was hurt. Hobbs signs at ten past seven — one line out of four, and she says the word Monday out loud in January and nobody has produced a reason to move it.** **Five on the back of his left hand in biro, written with his right hand just after the barriers came down, and it is the correct number of door numbers and it is not in any document and he is not going to put it in one.**

### Chapter 140 — What He Did Not Write Down

Wednesday, day one hundred and twenty-three, the seventh of January 2043. Cliff ninety-three, Della one hundred and twenty-three, the man behind the wall one hundred and twenty-three. **The morning after, about three hours' sleep, nineteen people in the front room at nine, and nobody came to hear him.** He tells the room what happened in the words it happened in, standing up, and puts his left hand out palm up with a five on it in biro, and asks whether anybody wants to tell him tonight that the number of doors is what this district is going to be counted in for the next three weeks. **Nobody answers and one woman says *no* under her breath, and that is the whole of the consultation.** **Inez reads the vote and the four minutes in Petar's book and refuses to merge his lamp with the building's** — two different lamps, and the day they merge is the day the float is her decision and not six people's. **Cliff, at ninety-three days, asks the one question: was the man who held the frame the man who counted the doors — and says that somebody is going to have to write that down one day and it is not going to be any of us, and that he noticed, and that he is not bitter about it.** **Joon Park says the first night is the first night since 27 December with nothing on his list of the four things this building could have done and did not do, that it is an absence and not a win, and that it is the only thing that has happened in this district in four weeks, and he says it in a room on purpose.** **Then he writes one line in the notebook** — the four men, the ladder, five door numbers, eleven hours and ten minutes, nobody hurt, a door frame and a lamp that cost a pound and something, nothing in any document, nobody asked him to be there and nobody refused him — **and leaves five things out: the two times with nothing between them, the forty-four in a tube, the number on a nail against the number in a woman's mouth, a sentence at a bollard on Saturday, and a page in a folder in the Meridian with a copy in it that cannot be checked.** The reason is not a rule and not a promise: a woman in a room in December told him he had brought her a piece of paper she could not use, and on Tuesday night he found out what the other half of that is worth, because he had a genuine fact and nobody in the world he was entitled to give it to, and he had wanted to, and had not. **The tenth is a Saturday and it is Rennard and Murrow and a crew of five, and the fourth night is a Monday because a booking is a booking, and he has been on one of the four and has three to go.**

### Chapter 141 — It Failed In Front Of Everybody

Thursday, day one hundred and twenty-four, the eighth of January 2043. Cliff ninety-four, Della one hundred and twenty-four, the man behind the wall one hundred and twenty-four. **A working day inside the booking, and nothing in the district is shut for it.** A tower scaffold goes up on the verge at the low end of Rennard because a crew has been told to put one up, and a low-loader comes in over the soft road at 08:50. **The task sheet was handed to Peart at 08:05, out of a car, by a woman from the cell, and it is the tenth of January's row taken out of a column headed NIGHT, and there is no field on it for a morning because there has never been a morning.** **At 11:20 an outrigger goes a foot into soft ground, eight feet of scaffold comes across a pavement into the garden wall at number twenty-four, and eleven people are hurt. The man of sixty-one holding the ladder and the woman of forty-three holding the door frame at twenty-six are the two who are not on the clinic's list; the other nine are.** Mara is out at 12:05 and does the work she is for; the works' accident form goes in at 14:10 with eleven names on it; Rhoda Mistry comes down the hill, reads the eleven names off the form taped to the barrier, and says nothing. **Hobbs shuts the daylight operation at 11:26 and writes the reason on the works' stop sheet in her own hand, which she has not done since 2036.** He was nine feet away and catches a child, and there is no gift and no hand and he had one right hand and it was holding a child. **The failure is in the cell's own day book**, a hardback with one ruled line a day on a shelf behind the counter that nobody had ever mentioned, in **T. Ayodele's** own hand at the end of the afternoon, and it is not the verification log, not the counter log, not the clearance record and not the shift book: `08:05 task sheet to works, Rennard day block, per OP/1 row 2, no time on it, T. Ayodele. 09:12 counter open, Tallow, nothing said. 11:20 incident works crew Rennard, 11 persons hurt.` **Anum infers from having never been asked to write in it that it is not kept, and the book refutes him on the page — *it went to four o'clock, it was read out in the room, and the method officer's August went with it*. Anum says the team has been asking the right office the right question about the wrong book since December, and a reading has no field for the morning.** **Peart spends the hinge, once, in his own mouth: the terminal takes a correction, it holds the old figure and the new one and the man and the hour, and it cannot show what it said at ten past six this morning, because nobody built it to be asked what it said yesterday.** And the reason nine were on that street is that a works card carries a name, a night, a floor and a stair and **has no field for a street**, so the eleven could not be located from the works' own record at all, and nine of them were on Rennard and he could not have known. **He does not make it into a defense.** At half past six, nineteen people in the Ash Street front room, and Elias stands up and says he asked a woman in a room on 23 December to put her hands on eleven people, and she said no and she was right, and he is the only person who has done anything wrong in this. Mara says that is the correct sentence and she will not add to it. **Joon Park says the number is eleven on two pieces of paper made separately in two buildings and there is no form that says one of them had to be, and asks nobody to call it anything.**

### Chapter 142 — She Would Have To Say Yes

Friday, day one hundred and twenty-five, the ninth of January 2043. Cliff ninety-five, Della one hundred and twenty-five, the man behind the wall one hundred and twenty-five. **Joon Park says on the bus that he does not think she will say yes**, and that every reason she has for saying no is eleven years old and has never once let her down. At 22 Cutbank Row, in a two-room flat with a boiler that moves furniture, **she has had a letter: two hundred and six printed verification cards went out on Wednesday 6 January, with a photograph of a smiling woman, the words *your interests protected*, a box for a signature and a line reading *document surrendered for check*, and she has sent nothing and says so in front of both of them so that neither can report it.** **She puts the sheet down folded in three and tells him to ask properly, and he does.** She says yes in a small way: **two of the four.** The two she gives are the two a person on that row can still say out loud; the other two have nobody left on this side of the cut and she will not put the name of a person she cannot stand behind on her own road onto a sheet that goes on a nail in a shop. **She reads all four out loud once in her own kitchen and neither man writes anything down: Meredith Prothero, 81, of 3 Sallow Court · Gwilym Rees, 66, of 11 · Nesta Morgan, 79, of 19 · Aneurin Pugh, 58, of 27, who was the top of the block, whose van was outside the door on the eighth and not on the ninth, and about whom she will say one thing more and it is that she wrote his name down.** **Then the volume's one permitted carrying-beat: Joon Park is in the shop opposite and does not come out, and a woman of about thirty with a folder and a good coat comes up the stairs with one of the two hundred and six cards, and Alagoa refuses it in her own doorway, to two people, on a reason about her street — that nobody has asked her one thing in eleven years, that the sheet is on her table and is going to stay on her table, that she knows what it is to be asked to surrender something in a doorway, and that it is not about your office — and sends her down the stairs, and the woman reports it at the counter of number six like the weather, and Joon says thank you and asks nothing and writes nothing down and Elias does not write it down either, and nobody says a word about it then or afterwards.** Last of all, **the depot letter of 9 January**, in full: from 14 January the Friday hose certification moves to Thursday 06:30, the Tuesday is unchanged, inventory remains as notified, slots will not be held twice, and the notice does not alter, renew or replace any other entitlement of the holder. **It is not about him, and it takes nothing away and gives nothing else, and the Tuesday at half past six still costs him an hour of sleep a week for the rest of his life.**

### Chapter 143 — The Second Of The Four Nights

Saturday, day one hundred and twenty-six, the tenth of January 2043. Cliff ninety-six, Della one hundred and twenty-six, the man behind the wall one hundred and twenty-six. **The counter is shut behind a locked door, the board is shut, the yard is open, and that is the whole of the week.** Twenty-two to thirty-two Rennard and sixteen to eighteen Murrow, crew of five, and it is the third comparison in five days and the first time anybody has made the three versions agree. **Five conditions go up on Peart's own board in his own hand and Hobbs changes none of them: no scaffold off the bank of the Cut, no vehicle on the soft road at any hour until the board's engineer says so in writing, no daylight work in any block with a date on it, a ladder for every stair, and nobody's name on a sheet except the crew's own. The third is Peart's, the fourth is Doy's, the second is the driver's.** The low-loader will not go down Rennard, so everything comes down on five backs. **The stair at 26 is half out, because the make safe was stopped at 11:26 on Thursday with three men on it, and a woman of eighty-two lives above it.** Doy says nobody is going down that with a lamp; Hobbs says it has to be done tonight or at eight o'clock tomorrow morning a woman of eighty-two is in a house with no stair in it, and answers *you did that stair on Thursday morning in daylight with a woman standing on it* with the eleven names on a form in a yard, and stands under it herself. **She comes down at 20:20, one step every forty seconds, with a man's hand under her elbow and a second on the rail, and he goes down backwards in front of her with a lamp in his right hand and his left in his coat, and it takes eleven minutes and nobody says anything.** She asks to stand in the road and watches five men carry her furniture past a boarded-up garden wall, with a bandage on her wrist, and he carries a chest of drawers past her with his right shoulder. **Nobody is hurt.** Eleven hours and twenty minutes, first door at half past seven to the barriers down at ten to seven, and nothing goes over the soft road at any point. **A man of sixty-one is on a ladder at two in the morning and Peart says he is sixty-three and put himself there, and that he has stopped asking people what they can do and started asking them what they would rather do.** **Hobbs signs the second of four lines at about ten past seven on the Sunday morning, and says she has said the word NIGHT out loud twice in six days and has not once said the other one.** He writes one line and a second line, and the second line is that the same eleven were not hurt tonight for reasons that are five men and a foreman and a road and nothing to do with a room. **At half past eight on Sunday morning there are two documents on the yard gate instead of one: the night schedule, and the accident form with eleven names on it, stringed to the gate, with *11 injured — 9 home — no further injuries on 10 Jan.* written under the last line, and it is the only document in thirteen weeks that has ever put those eleven names in the same place as those eleven people.**

### Chapter 144 — Three People, No Notice, Every Office Shut

Sunday, day one hundred and twenty-seven, the eleventh of January 2043. Cliff ninety-seven, Della one hundred and twenty-seven, the man behind the wall one hundred and twenty-seven. **Four ruled lines on Hobbs's sheet, two signed, two blank, and she was right to sign the second one at ten past seven rather than at half past six.** The yard is shut because a yard shuts on Sundays, the counter is shut, the board is shut, the hearing room is shut, and there is no opening anywhere in the city on Sunday the eleventh of January except a shop that sells door furniture and a newsagent that shuts at one. **At about a quarter past ten on Cutbank Row he finds three people at the end of the row with a bag each and nobody talking to them, and nothing has come — no notice, nothing in the air on the wall at the top of the row, nothing in his hands.** He stops four feet away and asks two questions and tells them that is all he is going to ask today. **Emrys Vaughan, 41, of a flat in a building on a street that is not in this district · Marek Drzewicki, 19, of an address eleven minutes from the bollard · Sabina Hough, 63.** **Alagoa comes out and tells him not to ask the third question, and that there is nowhere on a Sunday to take three people, and that what he has is two questions' worth of writing down and an arrangement with a day on it.** **Piotr Drzewicki, 54, comes up the row at a speed that is not a walking speed, says a word in Polish, and says *you have his name* — and has been to the office four times and cannot make the man behind the counter put a name on a birth certificate, and every time the man has shown him the same page and said *sir, this is what we hold*, and it is not a mistake, and that is the whole of what is wrong with it.** **Emrys says he has wanted somebody in an office to tell him he is wrong since the Wednesday before last, that two people have done it today and it has not made him wrong, and that his wife is called Bronwen and he has said that name out loud on this pavement twice and it is the wrong name for this city and he would like somebody to write it down and tell him in a month it is on nothing.** **Declan Hough, 31, arrives at ten past eleven and says he is her brother's son, that he went to her funeral in 2039, and that he will do whatever is put in front of him and is not going to decide anything today.** Nobody speaks for about a minute. **Nobody asks any of the three a single question about what they want**, they are walked into the newsagent because Mrs Postridge lets them stand out of the wind on the strength of nothing at all, and Alagoa goes back in and shuts her door eight inches and then opens it another four.

### Chapter 145 — What Is Your Name And What Is Your Address

Monday, day one hundred and twenty-eight, the twelfth of January 2043. Cliff ninety-eight, Della one hundred and twenty-eight, the man behind the wall one hundred and twenty-eight. **The first working day in the batch, and the only two questions the volume is permitted to ask anybody were asked at ten past ten.** The district's arrival book, twenty-six years on a shelf in the board's third floor, came down to Cutbank Row on Friday. **Garnet Okpara, about fifty, nineteen years at the counter, says in the first four minutes and in front of the visitors that there is nothing in front of him that is not true, and that the three of them have been told by at least three people there is something wrong with their papers and there is not — a certificate of marriage, a register of births, a tenancy, every page signed by somebody alive and entitled to sign it, and the difficulty is that two of them are true about a world that is not this one and he is not able to say which, and if he could he would have done it in a morning.** Elias gets as far as asking a third question and stops himself in front of them. **Marek's receipt from Wednesday the fourth of March goes into a folder of Okpara's own with the date on it, which is not a form and is not in any book, and which he says he did for a man in 1939 as well.** The birth register for that year has two hundred and forty-one entries and none of them is Marek, and what Okpara can do is enter him, at
